  - "Now, when your physical strength is gone, your wealth is useless, and the punishment is choking your throat, you claim faith?!"
- **The Contrast with Past Insolence (*Wa-qad Kuntum bihi Tastaʿjilūn*):**
  - The past perfect continuous phrase *wa-qad kuntum bihi tastaʿjilūn* highlights their former arrogance: "The very punishment you spent years taunting and daring God to unleash has now arrived—why are you screaming in terror instead of celebrating?"

**Classical Exegesis & The Theology of Valid Faith (Īmān al-Ikhtiyār vs. Īmān al-Iḍṭirār)**

Classical theologians, including al-Ṭabarī, Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, Ibn Kathīr, and al-Qurṭubī, establish the fundamental theological principle governing the validity of repentance and faith:
- **Faith by Free Choice (*Īmān al-Ikhtiyār*):** Belief is meritorious, spiritually transformative, and accepted by God only when it is chosen freely during the period of testing (*al-Ghayb*)—when a person uses reason, conscience, and revelation to submit to the Creator without physical coercion.
- **Forced Faith Under Duress (*Īmān al-Iḍṭirār*):** The moment physical punishment strikes or the veil of the physical world is torn away by the Angel of Death, the realm of the Unseen becomes the realm of direct physical sight (*al-Shahādah*). At that moment, belief is no longer a moral virtue; it is merely an involuntary submission to overwhelming physical evidence. The examination period has terminated, and the door of repentance is permanently locked.

**The Haunting Parallel: The Drowning of Pharaoh in Verse 91**

Classical commentators note that this exact divine reprimand—*āl-ān!* ("Now?!")—will be addressed directly to Pharaoh in verse 91 of this very Sūrah, when he is drowning in the churning waters of the Red Sea. The Qur'an demonstrates the identical psychology connecting all tyrants and mockers across human history.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 51 provides a profound warning against spiritual procrastination:
1. **The Trap of 'I Will Repent Later':** Many people acknowledge the truth of God and moral law, but delay their repentance, thinking: *"I will enjoy worldly sins during my youth, and when I am old or sick, I will start praying and repent."* Verse 51 exposes the lethal flaw in this calculation: you cannot guarantee when your *Ajal* (death) will strike, and last-minute repentance made under the terror of death is rejected.
2. **The Integrity of Living for Truth Today:** True wisdom consists of embracing truth while you possess health, free will, and the capacity to act righteously.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:52

> **Then it will be said to those who did wrong, “Taste the eternal punishment! Are you recompensed for aught save that which you used to earn?”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 52 delivers the final, irrevocable judicial sentencing pronounced upon persistent oppressors and unrepentant deniers on the Day of Judgment: *"Then it will be said to those who did wrong, 'Taste the eternal punishment! Are you recompensed for aught save that which you used to earn?'"* (*Thumma qīla li'lladhīna ẓalamū dhūqū ʿadhāba al-khuldi hal tujzawna illā bimā kuntum taksibūn*).

In verse 51, their desperate last-minute conversion was rejected; here, the divine court passes the ultimate sentence. The verse establishes the eternal duration of retribution for persistent injustice (*al-Ẓulm*) while reaffirming the absolute mathematical fairness and equity of divine recompense.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Anatomy of Eternal Retribution**

The linguistic construction conveys solemn judicial finality:
- **The Sensory Imperative of Retribution (*Dhūqū*):** The imperative verb *dhūqū* ("Taste!") emphasizes the direct, intimate, and physical reality of the punishment. It is not an abstract legal concept; it is an immediate, sensory experience that penetrates the soul and body.
- **The Definition of *ʿAdhāb al-Khuld* (Eternal Punishment):**
  - *Al-Khuld* denotes perpetual, unbroken, and everlasting duration without termination.
  - The punishment is designated as *ʿAdhāb al-Khuld* because it is the eternal consequence of a soul that made an eternal choice to reject truth.
- **The Rhetorical Interrogative of Mathematical Justice (*Hal Tujzawna Illā...*):**
  - The interrogative *hal* combined with the restrictive *illā* forms an absolute rhetorical confirmation: "Are you being recompensed for anything other than the exact harvest of what your own hands cultivated?"
  - The verb *taksibūn* denotes deliberate, accumulative worldly acquisition. The Hellfire is not an external arbitrary infliction; it is the ontological manifestation of the sinner's own life choices.

**Classical Exegesis & The Philosophical Justification of Eternal Punishment**

A classic theological question addressed by scholars like al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī, al-Ghazālī, Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, and Ibn Kathīr is: *Why is a finite lifespan of sin on earth punished with an eternal duration in the Fire?*
- **The Principle of Permanent Internal Intention (*al-Niyyah al-Abadiyyah*):**
  - Imam al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī famously explained that the people of Paradise abide in Paradise eternally because their settled intention was to obey God forever as long as they lived, while the people of the Fire abide in the Fire eternally because their settled intention was to persist forever in disbelief and rebellion as long as they lived.
  - Had these obstinate tyrants lived for millions of years on earth, their fundamental orientation toward truth, God, and justice would never have changed.
- **The Severity of the Transgression:** Sins are calibrated not merely by the physical seconds taken to commit them, but by the magnitude of the Being against Whom the crime is committed. Rejecting the Infinite, Eternal Creator and suppressing His eternal truth is a crime of infinite moral gravity.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 52 provides profound clarity on the relationship between earthly deeds and eternal consequences:
1. **The Harvest of the Soul:** The Qur'anic concept of *Kasb* (earnings) reminds humanity that every word spoken, every dollar acquired through fraud, every act of cruelty, and every moment of arrogance is an active deposit into one's spiritual account. On Judgment Day, God simply hands you the exact harvest of what you planted.
2. **The Urgency of Rectifying the Ledger:** The believer is called to inspect their earnings daily: *What am I earning today? Are my deeds building a legacy of light in the Garden, or am I accumulating spiritual debt that leads to the Fire?*

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:53

> **And they ask thee to inform them: “Is it true?” Say, “Yea, by my Lord! Truly it is the truth, and you cannot thwart [Him].”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 53 captures the underlying psychological anxiety and lingering doubts of the Makkan skeptics: *"And they ask thee to inform them: 'Is it true?' Say, 'Yea, by my Lord! Truly it is the truth, and you cannot thwart [Him]'* (*Wa-yastanbiʾūnaka a-ḥaqqun huwa qul ī wa-Rabbī innahu la-ḥaqqun wa-mā antum bi-muʿjizīn*).

Despite their public mockery, cynical bravado, and aggressive propaganda campaigns against the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ, deep within the quiet recesses of their conscience, the polytheistic leaders were haunted by his warnings: *Is the Resurrection real? Is there truly an eternal judgment? Is this Qur'an truly the Word of the Lord of the worlds?*

God commands the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ to answer their inquiry with the most solemn, emphatic, and decisive oath found in the entire sacred scripture.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Three Qur'anic Oaths of Resurrection**

The linguistic construction contains supreme evidential and rhetorical weight:
- **The In-Depth Inquiry (*Yastanbiʾūnaka*):** The Form X verb *istanbaʾa* denotes seeking serious, definitive, and consequential news (*Nabaʾ*). This was not casual curiosity; it was an anxious demand for final confirmation.
- **The Particle of Solemn Affirmation (*Ī*):** The word *ī* is an ancient, emphatic Arabic particle used exclusively before oaths to mean "Yes, indeed! Absolutely!"
- **The Solemn Divine Oath (*Wa-Rabbī* - "By My Lord!"):**
  - Classical scholars note that there are only **three places in the entire Qur'an** where God commands the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ to swear by His Divine Name to affirm the unshakeable reality of the Resurrection:
    1. Here in Sūrah Yūnus (10:53): *"Say: Yea, by my Lord! Truly it is the truth!"*
    2. In Sūrah Sabāʾ (34:3): *"Say: Yea, by my Lord! It will surely come upon you!"*
    3. In Sūrah al-Taghābun (64:7): *"Say: Yea, by my Lord! You will surely be resurrected!"*
- **The Impossibility of Escape (*Wa-mā Antum bi-Muʿjizīn*):**
  - The term *muʿjizīn* refers to those who can escape, incapacitate, outrun, or thwart an authority.
  - The prepositional *bāʾ al-zāʾidah* in the predicate (*bi-muʿjizīn*) provides absolute emphatic negation: you cannot escape outside the borders of God's cosmos, nor can you resist His cosmic summons when the trumpet sounds.

**Classical Exegesis & The Evidential Weight of the Prophetic Oath**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, unpack the psychological impact of this oath:
- The Makkans knew with absolute certainty that Muhammad ﷺ had never uttered a lie in his entire life—even before claiming prophethood.
- When a man of such unblemished integrity swears a solemn oath by his Creator (*Ī wa-Rabbī!*), it delivers an overwhelming psychological blow to the skeptic's doubts, forcing them to confront the reality of eternal judgment.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 53 provides an unshakeable anchor of certainty for modern faith:
1. **Confronting Modern Eschatological Skepticism:** Modern secular culture often dismisses the afterlife as an ancient myth, encouraging people to live as if death is total annihilation. Verse 53 meets this skepticism with absolute prophetic certitude: *Ī wa-Rabbī innahu la-ḥaqq!* The afterlife is not a metaphor or a wishful thought; it is an absolute, objective ontological reality.
2. **The Futility of Worldly Defenses:** No matter how wealthy, technologically insulated, or politically connected a human being may be, no bunker, cryogenics lab, or offshore wealth can thwart (*muʿjizīn*) the divine appointment with death and resurrection. The only rational response is to prepare for the meeting.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:54

> **And if every soul that has done wrong possessed all that is on the earth, it would ransom itself therewith. And they will conceal remorse when they see the punishment. And judgment will be rendered between them with justice, and they will not be wronged.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 54 depicts the overwhelming existential desperation, total economic bankruptcy, and psychological paralysis that will seize every oppressor on the Day of Judgment: *"And if every soul that has done wrong possessed all that is on the earth, it would ransom itself therewith. And they will conceal remorse when they see the punishment. And judgment will be rendered between them with justice, and they will not be wronged"* (*Wa-law anna li-kulli nafsin ẓalamat mā fī al-arḍi la'ftadat bihi wa-asarru al-nadāmata lammā raʾawu al-ʿadhāba wa-quḍiya baynahum bi'l-qisṭi wa-hum lā yuẓlamūn*).

In verse 53, the Prophet ﷺ swore by God that the Resurrection is undeniable truth; here, verse 54 reveals the total worthlessness of worldly wealth in the divine court, exposing how the greatest fortunes accumulated through corruption cannot buy a single second of relief from divine justice.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Total Rejection of Ransom**

The linguistic architecture of the verse provides profound psychological and legal dimensions:
- **The Ultimate Hypothetical (*Wa-law Anna li-Kulli Nafsin Ẓalamat...*):**
  - If a single wrongdoer possessed all the gold reserves, real estate, oil fields, corporate empires, and treasuries of planet Earth (*mā fī al-arḍ*), they would eagerly offer every single penny of it as a ransom (*Fidyah*) to escape a single moment of punishment.
  - In this world, money buys elite legal teams, bribes corrupt judges, and secures luxury bail. In the divine court, earthly wealth possesses zero currency value.
- **The Paralyzing Remorse (*Wa-Asarrū al-Nadāmah*):**
  - The verb *asarra* in Arabic carries two complementary meanings:
    1. **To Conceal/Internalize:** The remorse is so crushing, suffocating, and catastrophic that their vocal cords freeze; they are physically incapable of screaming, suffering in silent, internal spiritual paralysis.
    2. **To Manifest/Reveal:** Some classical linguists note that *asarra* is from the *Aḍdād* (words with opposite meanings), meaning their remorse becomes undeniably visible upon their shattered expressions.
- **The Guarantee of Impartial Equity (*Wa-Quḍiya Baynahum bi'l-Qisṭ*):**
  - Judgment is executed with absolute mathematical justice (*al-Qisṭ*). No innocent soul will be charged, no good deed will be forgotten, and no punishment will exceed the exact measure of the crime (*wa-hum lā yuẓlamūn*).

**Authentic Prophetic Traditions on the Worthlessness of Earthly Ransom**

The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ conveyed this terrifying reality in an authentic ḥadīth recorded in *Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī* and *Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim*:

> *"God will say to the person with the lightest punishment among the people of the Fire on the Day of Resurrection: 'If you possessed whatever is on the earth of wealth and treasures, would you ransom yourself with it?' He will reply: 'Yes, O my Lord!' God will say: 'I demanded of you something far easier than that when you were in the loins of Adam: that you associate nothing with Me, but you insisted upon polytheism!'"* (Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī 3334; Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim 2805, from Anas ibn Mālik)

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 54 provides an essential calibration of human values:
1. **The Real Value of Faith Today:** Today, in the testing ground of life, the price of salvation is effortless: believing in the One God, avoiding major sins, praying, and acting with justice. Tomorrow, on Judgment Day, the entire wealth of planet Earth will be rejected as insufficient. The wise soul buys their salvation today at the affordable price of humble faith.
2. **Liberation from Materialistic Obsession:** Human beings ruin their health, alienate families, and compromise moral integrity to amass temporary fortunes. Verse 54 exposes the ultimate destination of unrighteous wealth: on Judgment Day, you would gladly throw it all into the abyss for a single moment of peace.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:55

> **Lo! Truly unto God belongs whatsoever is in the heavens and the earth. Lo! Truly the promise of God is true. But most of them know not.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 55 establishes the supreme metaphysical and cosmological foundation for the entire preceding discussion: *"Lo! Truly unto God belongs whatsoever is in the heavens and the earth. Lo! Truly the promise of God is true. But most of them know not"* (*A-lā inna lillāhi mā fī al-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ a-lā inna waʿda Allāhi ḥaqqun wa-lākinna aktharahum lā yaʿlamūn*).

In verse 54, the Qur'an declared that even if a wrongdoer possessed all the wealth of the earth, it would not be accepted as a ransom. Verse 55 provides the ultimate metaphysical reason why ransom is impossible:
- Nothing in the universe belongs to human beings in the first place!
- Every galaxy, star, planet, mineral, and human soul is the absolute, unshared property (*Mulk*) of the Creator.
- Human ownership on earth is merely a brief, fiduciary loan—a temporary test of stewardship. You cannot ransom yourself to the True Sovereign using goods that already belong entirely to Him!

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Double Particles of Awakening (A-lā)**

The linguistic architecture of the verse utilizes double emphatic markers of cosmic certainty:
- **The Opening Particle of Awakening (*A-lā Inna Lillāhi...*):**
  - The particle *a-lā* is the *ḥarf istiftāḥ wa-tanbīh* (a particle of opening and urgent awakening), used to alert the mind to a monumental, non-negotiable reality.
  - Fronting the prepositional phrase (*lillāh*) establishes absolute, exclusive cosmic dominion: all matter, energy, space, and time belong exclusively to God.
- **The Second Particle of Awakening (*A-lā Inna Waʿda Allāhi Ḥaqq*):**
  - The repetition of *a-lā inna* reinforces the absolute infallibility of the Divine Promise (*Waʿd Allāh*).
  - Every promise made by God—the resurrection of the dead, the weighing of deeds, the eternal bliss of Paradise, and the retribution of the Fire—is an unshakeable ontological fact. Human skepticism does not delay it, and secular denial cannot alter it.
- **The Tragedy of Pervasive Ignorance (*Wa-lākinna Aktharahum Lā Yaʿlamūn*):**
  - The vast majority of humanity lives in a state of epistemological myopia—obsessed with the immediate sensory surface of worldly affairs while remaining completely blind to the deeper metaphysical realities governing existence.

**Classical Exegesis & The Definition of True Knowledge (Al-ʿIlm)**

Classical scholars and spiritual masters, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and al-Ghazālī, highlight the Qur'anic definition of authentic knowledge:
- In the Qur'anic worldview, *ʿIlm* (true knowledge) is not merely the technical memorization of worldly data, financial spreadsheets, or physical mechanics.
- True knowledge is recognizing the Creator, understanding the purpose of existence, and actively preparing for the eternal meeting with God.
- A person may hold multiple doctorates in astrophysics or economics, yet if they are oblivious to their Creator and unprepared for the Hereafter, the Qur'an categorizes them as: *lā yaʿlamūn* ("they know not").

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 55 provides a profound cure for human arrogance and existential anxiety:
1. **The Psychology of Detached Stewardship:** When a believer internalizes that all wealth, property, and children belong ultimately to God (*lillāhi mā fī al-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ*), their relationship with material possessions is revolutionized. They hold wealth in their hands without allowing greed to conquer their heart, sharing generously with the poor and accepting worldly losses with serene patience (*Innā lillāhi wa-innā ilayhi rājiʿūn*).
2. **Unshakeable Trust in Divine Promises:** When navigating personal hardships, societal oppression, or political injustice, the believer's heart is anchored in the absolute truth of God's promise: justice will prevail, truth will triumph, and the righteous will inherit eternal peace.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:56

> **He gives life and causes death, and unto Him you shall be returned.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 56 encapsulates the ultimate biological, metaphysical, and existential realities of human existence in four profound, majestic Arabic words: *"He gives life and causes death, and unto Him you shall be returned"* (*Huwa yuḥyī wa-yumītu wa-ilayhi turjaʿūn*).

Following the affirmation of divine cosmic ownership and the infallibility of the divine promise in verse 55, verse 56 brings the focus directly to the personal biological cycle of every human being: the initiation of life (*al-Iḥyāʾ*), the termination of life (*al-Imātah*), and the inevitable recall back to the Sovereign Creator (*al-Rujūʿ*).

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Monopolies of Life and Death**

The linguistic architecture of this verse provides profound theological boundaries:
- **The Form IV Verb *Yuḥyī* (He Gives Life):**
  - Derived from *ḥ-y-y*, *al-Iḥyāʾ* denotes breathing life, consciousness, and biological animation into inanimate matter.
  - Science can observe cellular mitosis, map the human genome, and describe chemical pathways, but no laboratory in human history can create a conscious living soul out of inorganic dust. Life is an uncreated, sovereign gift from the Living Creator (*al-Ḥayy*).
- **The Form IV Verb *Yumīt* (He Causes Death):**
  - Derived from *m-w-t*, *al-Imātah* denotes extracting the soul, terminating biological animation, and returning the body to dust.
  - Despite centuries of medical breakthroughs, anti-aging therapies, and pharmaceutical advancements, death remains an invincible, non-negotiable biological boundary. Every living organism must submit to its appointed deadline (*al-Ajal*).
- **The Exclusive Return (*Wa-Ilayhi Turjaʿūn*):**
  - In Arabic syntax, fronting the prepositional phrase (*wa-ilayh* - "and unto Him alone") before the passive verb (*turjaʿūn* - "you shall be returned") establishes exclusivity and inevitability.
  - Life is the deployment into the testing arena; death is the recall back to headquarters for accounting.

**Classical Exegesis & The Cycle of Existence**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, explain that these two attributes (*al-Muḥyī* and *al-Mumīt*) demonstrate the absolute uniqueness of God:
- No mortal king, tyrant, doctor, or idol can grant a single second of authentic life or escape the reality of death.
- The One Who initiates biological animation out of nothingness is self-evidently capable of resurrecting the body for the final return (*wa-ilayhi turjaʿūn*).

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 56 provides the ultimate foundation for human psychological liberation and moral clarity:
1. **Liberation from the Fear of Mortals:** When a human being internalizes that life and death are exclusively in the hand of God, they are liberated from fear of tyrants, employers, and oppressors. No human being possesses the power to shorten your life by a single second or withhold your sustenance without divine permission. The believer stands with dignity, fearing only God.
2. **The Inevitability of the Final Return:** Death is not the end of consciousness; it is the transition to the ultimate reality. Every path in the cosmos leads inevitably to God's courtroom. Living with this awareness transforms daily choices, inspiring the soul to live with justice, compassion, and continuous moral vigilance.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:57

> **O mankind! There has come unto you an exhortation from your Lord, a cure for that which is in the breasts, a guidance and a mercy for the believers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 57 delivers a magnificent, universal proclamation addressed to the entire human race (*Yā ayyuhā al-nās*), unveiling the fourfold divine dimensions, transformative power, and psychological healing embodied in the Holy Qur'an: *"O mankind! There has come unto you an exhortation from your Lord, a cure for that which is in the breasts, a guidance and a mercy for the believers"* (*Yā ayyuhā al-nāsu qad jāʾatkum mawʿiẓatun min Rabbikum wa-shifāʾun limā fī al-ṣudūri wa-hudan wa-raḥmatun li'l-muʾminīn*).

Having established the cosmic sovereignty of God, the fragility of worldly life, and the reality of the ultimate return, the divine discourse presents the supreme remedy provided by the Creator to heal the existential fractures of the human soul.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Fourfold Divine Dimensions of the Qur'an**

The verse outlines four progressive, interconnected spiritual stages through which the Qur'an transforms human consciousness:

1. **An Exhortation from Your Lord (*Mawʿiẓatun min Rabbikum*):**
   - The term *Mawʿiẓah* denotes an affectionate, moving, and powerful admonition that awakens sleeping consciences, softens hardened hearts, warns against destructive pitfalls, and reminds the soul of its divine origin and destiny.
2. **A Cure for That Which is in the Breasts (*Shifāʾun Limā fī al-Ṣudūr*):**
   - The Qur'an is the ultimate metaphysical, psychological, and spiritual medicine (*al-Shifāʾ*).
   - It penetrates directly into the human breast (*al-Ṣadr* / *al-Qalb*), purging two catastrophic categories of diseases:
     - **Epistemological Diseases (*Amrāḍ al-Shubuhāt*):** Skepticism, doubt, confusion, nihilism, superstition, and philosophical despair.
     - **Moral and Psychological Diseases (*Amrāḍ al-Shahawāt*):** Arrogance (*kibr*), envy (*ḥasad*), malice (*ghill*), greed (*ṭamaʿ*), obsessive materialism, and existential anxiety.
3. **A Guidance (*Hudā*):**
   - A flawless, perfectly calibrated compass and roadmap that illuminates every dimension of human life—creed, ethics, family relations, economic justice, social governance, and spiritual development.
4. **A Mercy for the Believers (*Raḥmatun li'l-Muʾminīn*):**
   - While the Qur'an is an exhortation and cure offered to all mankind, it becomes an active, boundless ocean of divine mercy (*Raḥmah*) for those who choose to believe. It shields them from existential despair in this world and guarantees them eternal salvation in Paradise.

**Classical Exegesis & The Holism of Qur'anic Healing**

Imam Ibn al-Qayyim writes eloquently in *Zād al-Maʿād*:

> *"The Qur'an is the complete cure for all spiritual and psychological ailments, as well as physical diseases. But not everyone is granted the qualification and divine guidance to seek healing through it. If the sick person treats their disease with the Qur'an with absolute certainty, sincere faith, complete acceptance, and firm conviction, no disease can ever resist it."*

Imam Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī notes that the sequence of the four descriptors represents the complete journey of human purification: *Mawʿiẓah* purifies the outer actions, *Shifāʾ* purifies the inner heart, *Hudā* illuminates the intellect with truth, and *Raḥmah* elevates the entire being into eternal divine grace.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 57 provides the definitive diagnosis and cure for the modern mental health and existential crisis:
1. **Healing Modern Existential Emptiness:** Modern secular culture possesses advanced medical technology and consumer luxuries, yet suffers from unprecedented rates of depression, anxiety, addiction, and loneliness. The Qur'an reveals why: human technology treats the physical body, but the soul (*al-Ṣadr*) can only be healed by the Word of its Creator. Reciting and internalizing the Qur'an restores inner serenity (*Sakīnah*) and existential peace.
2. **The Qur'an as Daily Therapy:** The believer does not approach the Qur'an merely as a ritual text, but as living divine medicine. Opening the Qur'an daily is an active session of spiritual therapy that cleanses toxic worldly thoughts and recharges the soul with divine light.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:58

> **Say, “In the bounty of God and in His mercy—in that let them rejoice; it is better than that which they amass.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 58 establishes the true, eternal criterion of human celebration, joy, and worth: *"Say, 'In the bounty of God and in His mercy—in that let them rejoice; it is better than that which they amass'"* (*Qul bi-faḍli Allāhi wa-bi-raḥmatihi fa-bi-dhālika fal-yafrahū huwa khayrun mimmā yajmaʿūn*).

In verse 57, the Qur'an presented itself as the supreme exhortation, cure, guidance, and mercy from the Lord of the worlds. Here, verse 58 commands the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ to establish what truly deserves human jubilation (*al-Faraḥ*).

Human beings routinely celebrate material achievements—financial promotions, luxury real estate, academic degrees, social status, and consumer toys. Verse 58 radically reorients human consciousness: true, dignified, and eternal joy belongs to the soul that has been honored with divine revelation, illuminated with faith (*Īmān*), and connected with the Creator.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The True Source of Joy**

The linguistic architecture of the verse emphasizes exclusivity and supreme comparative value:
- **The Definition of *Faḍl Allāh* (Bounty) and *Raḥmat Allāh* (Mercy):**
  - Classical exegetes, led by ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās, explain:
    > *"The bounty of God (Faḍl Allāh) is sacred knowledge (al-ʿIlm) and Islam, and His mercy (Raḥmatuh) is the Holy Qur'an and the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ."* (Tafsīr al-Ṭabarī; Tafsīr Ibn Kathīr)
  - Al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī and Mujāhid stated: *"The bounty of God is Islam, and His mercy is the Qur'an."*
- **The Exclusivity of Joy (*Fa-bi-Dhālika Fal-Yafrahū*):**
  - In Arabic syntax, the repetition of the prepositional demonstrative (*fa-bi-dhālika*) followed by the imperative *lām* (*fal-yafrahū*) establishes exclusivity: "In that divine gift alone—and not in transient worldly toys—let them celebrate with all their hearts!"
- **The Comparative Formula (*Huwa Khayrun mimmā Yajmaʿūn*):**
  - The verb *yajmaʿūn* denotes amassing, accumulating, and hoarding worldly assets—real estate, gold, stocks, and consumer goods.
  - The verse declares that a single verse of the Qur'an understood and integrated into the heart, a single sincere prostration before God, and a single moment of connection with the Divine is infinitely superior to the entire amassed GDP of human history.

**Classical Exegesis & The Historical Paradigm of Righteous Joy**

Classical historians record a profound historical moment during the Caliphate of ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb:
- When the massive wealth, gold, and jewels from the conquest of the Persian Empire were brought to Medina and placed before ʿUmar, his servant exclaimed with joy: *"O Commander of the Faithful! This is indeed the bounty of God and His mercy!"*
- ʿUmar immediately corrected him, saying:
  > *"You have lied! That is not what God meant in this verse. Rather, this wealth is what people amass (mā yajmaʿūn), whereas the bounty of God and His mercy is Islam, the Qur'an, and righteous guidance!"* (Tafsīr al-Ṭabarī; Tafsīr Ibn Kathīr)

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 58 provides the ultimate antidote to modern consumerist dissatisfaction and social comparison:
1. **The Psychology of Authentic Joy:** Modern consumerism operates on the manufactured lie that happiness is found in accumulating more material goods (*mimmā yajmaʿūn*). Yet, endless accumulation leads only to the hedonic treadmill—temporary excitement followed by renewed emptiness and anxiety. Verse 58 reveals the secret of enduring joy: anchor your self-worth and happiness in your spiritual connection with God and His revelation.
2. **True Spiritual Dignity:** Even if a believer lives in material simplicity or experiences financial hardship, they walk with immense spiritual dignity and inner joy, knowing that they possess the greatest treasure in existence: the Light of the Qur'an and the Mercy of Islam.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:59

> **Say, “Have you considered that which God has sent down for you of provision, whereof you have made [some] forbidden and [some] lawful?” Say, “Did God give you leave, or do you fabricate against God?”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 59 confronts one of the most widespread, dangerous forms of religious corruption and theological usurpation across human history: the arbitrary, unauthorized legislation of lawful (*Ḥalāl*) and unlawful (*Ḥarām*) matters: *"Say, 'Have you considered that which God has sent down for you of provision, whereof you have made [some] forbidden and [some] lawful?' Say, 'Did God give you leave, or do you fabricate against God?'"* (*Qul a-raʾaytum mā anzala Allāhu lakum min rizqin fa-jaʿaltum minhu ḥarāman wa-ḥalālān qul Āllāhu adhina lakum am ʿalā Allāhi taftarūn*).

In pre-Islamic Arabian paganism, the Quraysh and surrounding tribes invented complex dietary taboos and animal consecration rituals based on superstition. They designated certain camels and cattle (*al-Baḥīrah*, *al-Sāʾibah*, *al-Waṣīlah*, *al-Ḥām*) as sacred, declaring that their milk or meat was forbidden to women, reserved exclusively for pagan priests, or dedicated to idols (as detailed in Qur'an 6:138–140).

Verse 59 dismantles this religious charlatanism, establishing the exclusive legislative sovereignty of God Almighty.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Devastating Dilemma**

The linguistic architecture of the verse confronts religious innovators with an inescapable logical trap:
- **The Divine Origin of Provision (*Mā Anzala Allāhu lakum min Rizq*):** The food, cattle, crops, and natural resources of the earth were sent down and created by God as a pure, generous provision (*Rizq*) for human sustenance.
- **The Usurpation of Authority (*Fa-Jaʿaltum minhu Ḥarāman wa-Ḥalālā*):** Mortals took this divine provision and, without revelation, began drawing lines—declaring some foods holy and others taboo.
- **The Inescapable Dialectical Dilemma (*Qul Āllāhu Adhina Lakum am ʿalā Allāhi Taftarūn*):**
  - God commands the Prophet ﷺ to demand their epistemic credentials:
    1. *Āllāhu adhina lakum* ("Did God give you leave/permission?"): Did God send down an authentic scripture or prophet granting you the legal authority to invent these prohibitions?
    2. *Am ʿalā Allāhi taftarūn* ("or do you fabricate lies against God?"): If you possess no divine revelation, then you are guilty of the gravest spiritual crime: speaking on behalf of God without authority and inventing lies against the Creator!

**Classical Exegesis & The Foundational Principle of Islamic Jurisprudence (Uṣūl al-Fiqh)**

Classical jurists and scholars, including Imam al-Shāfiʿī, Imam Aḥmad, al-Jaṣṣāṣ, and Ibn Kathīr, derive foundational jurisprudential principles from this verse:
1. **The Sovereign Right of Legislation (*al-Tashrīʿ*):** The right to declare something *Ḥalāl* (permissible) or *Ḥarām* (prohibited) belongs exclusively, absolutely, and unshared to God and His Messenger ﷺ.
2. **The Principle of Original Permissibility (*al-Aṣl fī al-Ashyāʾ al-Ibāḥah*):** In worldly matters, food, transactions, and natural provisions, the default baseline ruling is permissibility (*Ibāḥah*), unless an explicit, authentic divine text establishes a prohibition.
3. **The Prohibition of Religious Extremism and Innovation (*al-Bidʿah*):** Arbitrarily prohibiting what God made lawful is as grave a spiritual sin as permitting what God made unlawful. Both represent an insolent attempt to edit divine law.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 59 provides a crucial safeguard against cultic manipulation and self-righteous extremism:
1. **The Danger of Cultic Religious Leaders:** Throughout history, cult leaders, extremist preachers, and false gurus manipulate followers by inventing arbitrary prohibitions—forbidding lawful foods, normal social interactions, or lawful marriages—to exert psychological control over their devotees. Verse 59 establishes the eternal standard: demand verified divine proof (*Adillah Sharʿiyyah*) for every religious prohibition.
2. **The Balance and Mercy of Islam:** Islam is a religion of balance and natural harmony (*al-Dīn al-Qayyim*). It permits all pure, wholesome, and beneficial provisions (*al-Ṭayyibāt*) while prohibiting only that which is inherently harmful, filthy, or destructive to human well-being (*al-Khabāʾith*).

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:60

> **And what will those who fabricate lies against God think on the Day of Resurrection? Truly God is full of bounty for human beings, but most of them are not thankful.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 60 delivers a chilling, awe-inspiring question that forces religious charlatans, innovators, and polytheists to visualize their standing before the Supreme Judge: *"And what will those who fabricate lies against God think on the Day of Resurrection? Truly God is full of bounty for human beings, but most of them are not thankful"* (*Wa-mā ẓannu alladhīna yaftarūna ʿalā Allāhi al-kadhiba yawma al-qiyāmah inna Allāha la-dhū faḍlin ʿalā al-nāsi wa-lākinna aktharahum lā yashkurūn*).

Building directly upon the condemnation of unauthorized religious legislation in verse 59, verse 60 addresses the ultimate consequences awaiting those who alter divine religion, fabricate superstitions, ascribe partners or children to God, or mislead humanity in the name of the Divine.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Dread of the Final Audit**

The linguistic phrasing creates an intense sense of eschatological dread:
- **The Terrifying Rhetorical Interrogative (*Wa-mā Ẓannu Alladhīna...*):**
  - The interrogative *wa-mā ẓannu* ("And what do they think / imagine?") is an open-ended rhetorical question loaded with supreme dread.
  - What do those who lied about God imagine will happen to them when they stand stripped of power before the Almighty on the Day of Resurrection?
  - Do they think they will be overlooked? Do they think their fabricated idols or self-righteous rationalizations will protect them? Do they imagine the Lord of the worlds will pardon their insolent assault upon His divine truth?
- **The Boundless Divine Bounty (*Inna Allāha la-Dhū Faḍlin ʿalā al-Nās*):**
  - The particle *inna* combined with the emphatic *lām* (*la-dhū faḍl*) affirms the boundless, overwhelming generosity of God.
  - God's bounty is manifested in granting life, health, senses, and provision even to those who deny Him; in withholding immediate destruction; in granting sinners time to repent; and in revealing clear scriptures to guide humanity to eternal Paradise.
- **The Pervasive Tragedy of Ingratitude (*Wa-lākinna Aktharahum Lā Yashkurūn*):**
  - Despite this continuous downpour of divine favor, the vast majority of humanity (*aktharahum*) fails to express basic gratitude (*Shukr*), attributing their success to idols, chance, or personal ego.

**Classical Exegesis & The Gravity of Fabricating Religious Falsehoods**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, and Ibn Kathīr, highlight the supreme gravity of speaking falsely about God:
- Imam Ibn al-Qayyim remarks in *Madārij al-Sālikīn* that speaking about God without knowledge (*al-Qawl ʿalā Allāh bi-ghayri ʿIlm*) is the greatest and most dangerous of all sins, because it corrupts the very source of guidance and leads generations into spiritual ruin.
- The reckoning on Judgment Day for those who corrupted religion and misled souls will be severe beyond all human description.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 60 provides an essential moral compass for daily life:
1. **The Fear of Speaking Without Knowledge:** The sincere believer exercises extreme caution and humility when speaking about Islam, morality, and divine law. Never say "this is Ḥalāl" or "this is Ḥarām" based on casual personal feelings or cultural fashions; verify with sound scholarship, fearing the Day when you must answer before God for every word spoken.
2. **Breaking Away from the Ungrateful Majority:** The verse notes that most people fail to be thankful (*aktharahum lā yashkurūn*). The believer strives consciously to be among the grateful minority (*al-Qalīl al-Shākir*), beginning every day with heartfelt praise (*al-Ḥamdulillāh*) and using their health, wealth, and faculties in the service of their Creator.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:61

> **Thou art not [engaged] in any matter, nor dost thou recite any part of the Quran, nor do you [mankind] do any deed, but that We are Witnesses over you when you are engaged therein. And not so much as the weight of an atom escapes thy Lord, in the earth or in heaven, nor [anything] smaller than that or greater, but that it is in a clear Book.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 61 is one of the most awe-inspiring, comprehensive, and spiritually transformative passages in the entire Holy Qur'an, establishing the foundational bedrock for the spiritual station of *Murāqabah* (mindful divine vigilance and all-encompassing awareness). In the preceding verses (10:59–60), the Qur'an warned against fabricating falsehoods and usurping divine legislative authority; here, verse 61 unveils the terrifying and comforting reality of God's absolute, real-time omniscience and omnipresent observation over every microscopic vibration of the cosmos.

The verse addresses the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ directly in his personal worship and mission, transitions to the general actions of all humanity, and expands outward to the total physical inventory of subatomic and cosmic matter across the infinite heavens and the earth.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Four Tiers of Divine Observation**

The linguistic architecture of the verse unfolds across four meticulously structured clauses:

1. **The Specific Prophetic Engagement (*Wa-mā Takūnu fī Shaʾn*):**
   - The word *shaʾn* in classical Arabic denotes an affair of momentous significance, state, or circumstance.
   - Whether the Prophet ﷺ is leading the community, resolving legal disputes, or facing the hostility of enemies, God is directly watching.
2. **The Sacred Act of Qur'anic Recitation (*Wa-mā Tatlū minhu min Qurʾān*):**
   - The recitation of the Qur'an is singled out for honor: whenever the Prophet ﷺ or a believer stands in the quiet of the night reciting sacred scripture, the Lord of the Throne is actively listening and witnessing.
3. **The Universal Human Action (*Wa-lā Taʿmalūna min ʿAmal...*):**
   - The syntax shifts from the singular (*takūnu*, *tatlū*) to the plural (*taʿmalūna*, *tufīḍūna*), encompassing the entire human race.
   - *Idh tufīḍūna fīh* ("when you are engaged/immersed therein"): Derived from the root *f-y-ḍ* (which literally means water overflowing or flooding), the verb *tufīḍūna* describes diving deeply, being fully immersed, or rushing intensely into an action—whether a secret sin, a charitable deed, a business transaction, or a scientific discovery.
   - *Illā kunnā ʿalaykum shuhūdā* ("but that We are Witnesses over you"): God Almighty is *al-Shahīd*—the Omnipresent Witness Who observes the inception, the process, and the hidden motives behind every deed in real time.
4. **The Infinitesimal and the Subatomic (*Mithqāl Dharrah*):**
   - *Wa-mā yaʿzubu ʿan Rabbik* ("And not escapes your Lord"): The verb *yaʿzubu* denotes slipping away, remaining hidden, escaping notice, or being absent.
   - *Mithqāl dharrah* ("the weight of an atom/dust-mote"): In ancient Arabic, *al-dharrah* was the tiniest speck of dust visible in a sunbeam or a newborn ant; in modern physics, it is the atom.
   - *Wa-lā aṣghara min dhālika wa-lā akbar* ("nor anything smaller than that or greater"): An astonishing Qur'anic anticipation of subatomic reality—particles smaller than the atom (quarks, electrons, neutrinos) and structures larger than the atom—all indexed in the "Clear Book" (*Kitābin Mubīn* / *al-Lawḥ al-Maḥfūẓ*).

**Classical Exegesis & The Spiritual Station of Murāqabah**

Classical spiritual masters and exegetes, such as al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī, al-Muḥāsibī, Ibn Kathīr, and al-Qushayrī in *al-Risālah*, identify this verse as the supreme catalyst for spiritual excellence (*Iḥsān*):
- When a human being internalizes that God is *Shahīd* (Witness) over their every breath, keystroke, financial transaction, and private thought, their entire moral behavior is elevated.
- Sins committed in the dark are recognized not as "private acts," but as insolent transgressions committed directly in front of the Living Sovereign of the universe.
- Good deeds performed in secret without public applause are recognized as fully seen, cherished, and preserved by the Lord of the worlds.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 61 provides an indispensable antidote to modern alienation and moral duplicity:
1. **The Ultimate Cure for Loneliness:** In our hyper-connected yet deeply lonely modern world, human beings often feel unseen, unvalued, and spiritually isolated. Verse 61 offers sublime comfort: you are never alone; the Creator of the universe is actively witnessing your silent tears, your internal struggles, and your unexpressed prayers.
2. **The Ethics of the Digital Age:** In the internet era, where anonymity encourages cyberbullying, digital fraud, and secret consumption of obscenity, verse 61 is a piercing moral mirror: you can hide your screen from human eyes, but *Wa-mā yaʿzubu ʿan Rabbika mithqālu dharrah*—not an atom's weight escapes your Lord.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:62

> **Lo! Truly the friends of God—no fear shall come upon them, nor shall they grieve;**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 62 unveils one of the most sublime, comforting, and celebrated proclamations in the entire Holy Qur'an: the eternal security, transcendent majesty, and unshakeable peace granted to the beloved allies of the Divine: *"Lo! Truly the friends of God—no fear shall come upon them, nor shall they grieve"* (*A-lā inna awliyāʾa Allāhi lā khawfun ʿalayhim wa-lā hum yaḥzanūn*).

In verse 61, the Qur'an established God's all-encompassing observation over every human deed; here, verse 62 reveals the magnificent station of those whose lives are lived in continuous, loving awareness of that divine gaze: the **Awliyāʾ Allāh (The Friends and Allies of God)**.

The verse dismantles the two fundamental psychological afflictions that torment the human soul across history: existential fear of the future (*al-Khawf*) and crushing sorrow over the past (*al-Ḥuzn*).

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Grandeur of Divine Friendship (Wilāyah)**

The linguistic architecture of the verse carries supreme rhetorical and psychological depth:
- **The Opening Particle of Awakening (*A-lā Inna...*):** The combination of *a-lā* (awakening) and *inna* (certainty) alerts the listener to a monumental, unalterable metaphysical reality.
- **The Etymology of *Awliyāʾ* (Friends / Allies):**
  - *Awliyāʾ* is the plural of *Walī*, derived from the root *w-l-y*, which signifies nearness, intimacy, uninterrupted connection, loyalty, and deep mutual love.
  - A *Walī Allāh* is a servant who has drawn close to God through sincere faith, continuous obedience, remembrance, and the purification of their heart from worldly idolatry, thereby becoming an ally and beloved friend of the Lord of the worlds.
- **The Absolute Elimination of Fear (*Lā Khawfun ʿAlayhim*):**
  - Fear (*al-Khawf*) is an emotional agitation, anxiety, and dread concerning the **future**.
  - The friends of God are liberated from terror regarding what lies ahead: the agony of death, the darkness of the grave, the cosmic convulsions of the Day of Judgment, and the terror of the Hellfire.
- **The Absolute Elimination of Sorrow (*Wa-lā Hum Yaḥzanūn*):**
  - Sorrow (*al-Ḥuzn*) is an emotional despair, grief, and regret concerning the **past**.
  - The friends of God will experience zero sorrow or regret over the worldly wealth, status, or pleasures they sacrificed, because they will be submerged in the overwhelming ocean of divine grace and eternal reward.

**Authentic Prophetic Traditions on the Cosmic Station of the Awliyāʾ**

Imam Abū Dāwūd and Imam Aḥmad record a breathtaking, authentic ḥadīth transmitted by ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb, wherein the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ expounded directly upon this verse:

> *"Verily, among the servants of God are people who are neither prophets nor martyrs, yet the prophets and martyrs will envy their high station before God on the Day of Resurrection."* The Companions asked in awe: *"O Messenger of God, tell us who they are!"* He replied: *"They are people who loved one another for the sake of God, without any kinship between them and without any financial wealth they exchanged. By God, their faces are light, and they are upon light! They will not fear when people fear, nor will they grieve when people grieve."* Then he recited this verse: *"Lo! Truly the friends of God—no fear shall come upon them, nor shall they grieve."* (Sunan Abī Dāwūd 3527; Musnad Aḥmad 22967, graded ṣaḥīḥ)

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 62 provides the ultimate psychological anchor for navigating modern life:
1. **The Ultimate Cure for Anxiety and Depression:** Modern human beings are crushed by chronic anxiety about the future (career, health, financial stability) and deep depression over the past (failures, losses, regrets). Verse 62 reveals that true, unshakeable mental peace is not found in financial accumulation or secular pharmaceuticals; it is found in cultivating *Wilāyah*—intimate friendship and alliance with the Lord of the worlds.
2. **Living with Transcendent Fearlessness:** When a believer knows that the King of the universe is their *Walī* (Protector and Friend), they are liberated from fear of worldly tyrants, societal mobs, and economic downturns. They walk through life with serene, quiet courage.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:63

> **those who believe and are reverent.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Following the majestic proclamation of divine friendship in verse 62, verse 63 provides the definitive, objective, and universal theological definition of who the *Awliyāʾ Allāh* truly are: *"those who believe and are reverent"* (*Alladhīna āmanū wa-kānū yattaqūn*).

This concise, two-part verse is one of the most important theological texts in Islam. It completely de-mystifies, democratizes, and purifies the concept of spiritual sainthood (*Wilāyah*) from pagan superstitions, esoteric charlatanism, and elitist monopolies, establishing two universal, indispensable pillars accessible to every sincere human soul: Sound Faith (*Īmān*) and Continuous God-Consciousness (*Taqwā*).

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Two Pillars of Wilāyah**

The grammatical structure of the verse contains profound theological precision:
1. **Pillar 1: Sound, Unshakeable Faith (*Alladhīna Āmanū*):**
   - The verb *āmanū* in the past tense denotes a firm, settled foundation of correct Islamic creed (*ʿAqīdah*): pure monotheism (*Tawḥīd*) unpolluted by any major or minor polytheism, complete conviction in God, His angels, revealed scriptures, messengers, the Day of Judgment, and Divine Decree.
2. **Pillar 2: Continuous, Dynamic God-Consciousness (*Wa-Kānū Yattaqūn*):**
   - Notice the deliberate use of the past continuous auxiliary *kānū* combined with the present-tense imperfect verb *yattaqūn*.
   - In Arabic grammar, this structure denotes a **permanent, enduring lifestyle of Taqwā**—not an occasional, momentary emotional surge, but a sustained, daily practice of:
     - Fulfilling all divine obligations (*al-Wājibāt*),
     - Striving in voluntary good deeds (*al-Nawāfil*),
     - Meticulously abstaining from the forbidden (*al-Muḥarramāt*),
     - Guarding the tongue, heart, eyes, and limbs from moral corruption.

**Classical Exegesis & Demolishing Superstitions Around Sainthood**

Classical theologians, led by Shaykh al-Islām Ibn Taymiyyah in his masterpiece *al-Furqān bayna Awliyāʾ al-Raḥmān wa-Awliyāʾ al-Shayṭān*, emphasize that *Wilāyah* is not determined by lineage, mystical robes, inherited titles, or the performance of supernatural parlor tricks:
- As Imam al-Shāfiʿī famously remarked:
  > *"If you see a person walking on water or flying through the air, do not judge them to be a friend of God until you measure their actions against the Qur'an and the Sunnah!"*
- Imam Ibn Kathīr writes in his exegesis of this verse:
  > *"Every person who is reverent and God-fearing (Taqī) is a friend and ally of God (Walī Allāh)!"*

**The Sacred Hadith of Divine Alliance (Ḥadīth al-Walī)**

Imam al-Bukhārī records in his *Ṣaḥīḥ* the monumental *Ḥadīth Qudsī* outlining how a servant ascends the ranks of *Wilāyah*:

> *"God Almighty says: 'Whosoever shows enmity to a friend of Mine (Walī), I declare war upon him! And My servant draws near to Me with nothing more beloved to Me than the duties I have obligated upon him; and My servant continues to draw near to Me with voluntary deeds (Nawāfil) until I love him. And when I love him, I become his hearing with which he hears, his sight with which he sees, his hand with which he strikes, and his foot with which he walks. If he asks Me, I surely give him; and if he seeks refuge in Me, I surely protect him!'"* (Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī 6502, from Abū Hurayrah)

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 63 provides immense hope and practical direction for every believer:
1. **The Democratization of Spiritual Excellence:** In many religious traditions, sainthood is restricted to monastic celibates or deceased figures canonized by human committees. In Islam, *Wilāyah* is open to the factory worker, the mother raising children, the corporate executive, the student, and the farmer—anyone who embodies sincere faith (*Īmān*) and moral integrity (*Taqwā*).
2. **The Roadmap for Daily Living:** You do not need to seek out mystical caves to become a *Walī* of God. Fulfill your daily prayers, eat lawful food, treat people with kindness, fulfill your promises, avoid gossip, and maintain mindfulness of God in your workplace—this is the path of divine friendship.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:64

> **For them are glad tidings in the life of this world and in the Hereafter. There is no altering the Words of God. That is the great triumph.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 64 outlines the extraordinary, multi-tiered spiritual and physical rewards guaranteed to the *Awliyāʾ Allāh* (the friends of God): *"For them are glad tidings in the life of this world and in the Hereafter. There is no altering the Words of God. That is the great triumph"* (*Lahumu al-bushrā fī al-ḥayāti al-dunyā wa-fī al-ākhirah lā tabdīla li-kalimāti Allāhi dhālika huwa al-fawzu al-ʿaẓīm*).

Following the definition of the friends of God in verses 62 and 63, verse 64 unveils the comprehensive nature of *al-Bushrā* (glad tidings), demonstrating that divine friendship yields immediate, palpable blessings in the temporal world before culminating in the ultimate ecstasy of Paradise.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Classical Exegesis: The Twofold Dimension of Glad Tidings**

Classical commentators and authentic prophetic traditions explain the rich, multi-layered dimensions of these glad tidings (*al-Bushrā*):

1. **Glad Tidings in the Life of This World (*fī al-ḥayāti al-dunyā*):**
   - **True, Righteous Dreams (*al-Ruʾyā al-Ṣāliḥah*):** The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ explained this directly when ʿUbādah ibn al-Ṣāmit asked him about this verse:
     > *"It is the righteous dream that a Muslim sees or is seen for him; it is one forty-sixth part of prophecy."* (Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim 2263; Jāmiʿ al-Tirmidhī 2275)
   - **Good Reputation and Love Among the Righteous (*al-Thanāʾ al-Ḥasan*):** When God loves a servant, He commands Gabriel to announce His love to the inhabitants of heaven, and acceptance and love for that servant are placed in the hearts of the people of the earth (Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī 3209).
   - **Inner Serenity and Peace (*al-Sakīnah*):** The profound tranquility of heart that shields the believer from existential anxiety amidst worldly storms.
   - **The Angelic Welcoming at the Threshold of Death:** As the soul prepares to depart the physical body, luminous angels descend with the perfumes of Paradise, saying: *"Do not fear and do not grieve, but receive the glad tidings of Paradise which you were promised!"* (41:30).

2. **Glad Tidings in the Hereafter (*wa-fī al-ākhirah*):**
   - Welcoming greetings by the archangels upon emerging from the grave (*Yawm al-Qiyāmah*).
   - Total security and shelter under the Shade of God's Throne on the Day of Judgment.
   - Receiving the book of deeds in the right hand.
   - Crossing the *Ṣirāṭ* with the speed of lightning directly into the eternal Gardens of Delight.

**The Immutable Permanence of Divine Decrees (Lā Tabdīla li-Kalimāt Allāh)**

The verse affirms: *"There is no altering the Words of God"* (*Lā tabdīla li-kalimāti Allāh*).
- God's promises, covenants, and decrees are absolute, unchangeable, and eternally reliable.
- No political tyranny, military power, or demonic conspiracy in the universe can nullify or alter what God has ordained for His righteous servants.

**The Ultimate Seal: That is the Great Triumph (Al-Fawz al-ʿAẓīm)**

The verse concludes: *"That is the great triumph"* (*Dhālika huwa al-fawzu al-ʿaẓīm*).
- Attaining divine friendship, experiencing peace in this world, safety at death, and eternal Paradise in the Hereafter is the supreme, unrivaled success compared to which all worldly fortunes pale into absolute insignificance.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 64 provides profound encouragement for daily spiritual endurance:
1. **The Reality of Righteous Dreams:** In our hyper-rationalist modern culture, dreams are often dismissed as meaningless neural static. Islam acknowledges that while most dreams are psychological reflections, God gifts the sincere believer with true, prophetic dreams (*al-Ruʾyā al-Ṣādiqah*) that provide guidance, warnings, and profound glad tidings (*Bushrā*).
2. **Living with High Spiritual Morale:** Believers facing societal hostility, economic hardship, or personal trials find immense strength in this verse: our victory is not speculative; it is guaranteed by the immutable Word of the Lord of the worlds.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:65

> **And let not their speech grieve thee. Truly all honor belongs to God. He is the Hearer, the Knower.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 65 delivers tender, direct divine solace and psychological fortitude to the heart of the Messenger of God ﷺ: *"And let not their speech grieve thee. Truly all honor belongs to God. He is the Hearer, the Knower"* (*Wa-lā yaḥzunka qawluhum inna al-ʿizzata lillāhi jamīʿan huwa al-samīʿu al-ʿalīm*).

The Makkan pagan aristocracy subjected the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ to relentless psychological warfare—calling him a madman (*majnūn*), a sorcerer (*sāḥir*), a poet (*shāʿir*), an imposter, and a cursed man cut off from descendants (*al-Abtar*). For a man of supreme sensitivity, pristine moral purity, and profound love for humanity, these vicious slanders caused immense emotional pain and grief.

God intervenes directly to soothe his heart, establish the metaphysical monopoly of honor (*al-ʿIzzah*), and remind him that divine justice is actively monitoring every word.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Metaphysical Monopoly of Honor**

The linguistic architecture of the verse provides profound emotional and theological boundaries:
- **The Divine Prohibition Against Grief (*Wa-lā Yaḥzunka Qawluhum*):**
  - The prohibitive *lām* (*lā al-nāhiyah*) coupled with the jussive verb *yaḥzunka* commands the Prophet ﷺ: "Do not allow their insults, mocking propaganda, and slander to penetrate your heart or weigh upon your chest!"
  - Their words are mere vibrations of air devoid of ontological reality or power to harm the truth.
- **The Absolute Monopoly Over Honor (*Inna al-ʿIzzata Lillāhi Jamīʿā*):**
  - *Al-ʿIzzah* in Arabic encompasses honor, power, invincibility, prestige, and sovereign dominance.
  - Fronting the prepositional phrase (*lillāh*) combined with the totalizing noun *jamīʿan* ("all of it entirely") establishes that *every single particle of honor* belongs exclusively to God.
  - Arrogant oppressors seek honor through wealth, tribal alliances, and political dominance. The Qur'an asserts that any honor sought outside of submission to God is an illusion that inevitably ends in humiliation and defeat.
- **The Dual Divine Names (*Huwa al-Samīʿu al-ʿAlīm*):**
  - God is *al-Samīʿ* (The All-Hearing), Who hears every word uttered by the mockers in their secret assemblies.
  - God is *al-ʿAlīm* (The All-Knowing), Who knows their corrupt intentions, their hidden fears, and their malicious conspiracies.

**Classical Exegesis & The Vindication of Prophetic Dignity**

Classical scholars, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, unpack the profound consolation embedded in this verse:
- The Prophet ﷺ does not need to avenge himself or waste energy engaging in insults. The Lord of the Throne is his defender and judge.
- God promised that He would elevate the Prophet's name (*Wa-rafaʿnā laka dhikrak*), while reducing his slanderers (like Abū Jahl and al-ʿĀṣ ibn Wāʾil) to cursed footnotes of historical disgrace.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 65 provides an essential psychological shield for modern believers, leaders, and activists:
1. **Immunity to Online Slander and Public Criticism:** In the modern social media landscape, anyone who stands for traditional Islamic values, modesty, and justice is routinely subjected to coordinated smear campaigns, mockery, and cancel culture. Verse 65 teaches emotional mastery: *do not let their speech grieve you!* Human opinions cannot diminish the honor that God has granted to faith.
2. **Seeking True Honor (Al-ʿIzzah):** Many Muslims compromise their values, dress, or religious practices in a desperate bid to gain social acceptance and approval from secular Western cultures. Verse 65 reminds us: *all honor belongs exclusively to God!* Seeking dignity by compromising divine principles leads only to spiritual humiliation.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:66

> **Lo! Truly unto God belongs whosoever is in the heavens and whosoever is in the earth. And those who call upon partners apart from God follow not [them]. They follow naught but conjecture, and they do but lie.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 66 delivers a sweeping, cosmic refutation of all forms of polytheistic intercession and creature worship: *"Lo! Truly unto God belongs whosoever is in the heavens and whosoever is in the earth. And those who call upon partners apart from God follow not [them]. They follow naught but conjecture, and they do but lie"* (*A-lā inna lillāhi man fī al-samāwāti wa-man fī al-arḍi wa-mā yattabiʿu alladhīna yadʿūna min dūni Allāhi shurakāʾa in yattabiʿūna illā al-ẓanna wa-in hum illā yakhruṣūn*).

Building upon the affirmation of divine ownership in verse 55 and divine honor in verse 65, verse 66 focuses specifically upon the **conscious, rational beings** of the cosmos—angels, prophets, saints, kings, and jinn—demonstrating that every single entity ever deified by human beings is nothing more than a created, subservient property of the Almighty Creator.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Rational Pronoun (Man)**

The linguistic architecture contains precise grammatical nuances:
- **The Deliberate Use of *Man* (Whosoever):**
  - In Arabic grammar, *mā* refers to non-rational things (matter, objects), while *man* refers specifically to rational, conscious beings (angels, humans, jinn).
  - In verse 55, God said: *lillāhi mā fī al-samāwāt* (all non-rational matter belongs to God).
  - Here in verse 66, God says: *lillāhi man fī al-samāwāti wa-man fī al-arḍ* (all **conscious beings** belong to God).
  - This explicitly includes Archangel Gabriel, Prophet Jesus, Ezra, Mary, and all exalted saints: they are not divine partners; they are created servants belonging entirely to God.
- **The Phantom Nature of False Partners (*Wa-mā Yattabiʿu... Shurakāʾ*):**
  - Classical grammarians, including al-Zamakhsharī and al-Rāzī, highlight a brilliant syntactic insight: polytheists claim they are following "partners with God," but in reality, there are no actual partners in existence for them to follow! The "partners" are non-existent fictions constructed purely in human imagination.
- **The Root *Kh-r-ṣ* (Conjecture and Fabrication):**
  - The verb *yakhruṣūn* denotes guessing blindly, speculating without evidence, estimating falsehoods, and fabricating myths. Polytheism is not built on rational deduction or divine scripture; it is a castle of baseless assumptions.

**Classical Exegesis & The Demolition of Creature Deification**

Classical exegetes, including al-Ṭabarī and Ibn Kathīr, explain that this verse cuts the roots of all polytheistic cults:
- If every angel in heaven and every prophet on earth is already the property and servant of God, how can a rational human being direct worship, sacrifices, and prayers to a servant instead of the Master?
- True monotheism (*Tawḥīd*) liberates humanity from subservience to creation, elevating the human soul to worship only the Uncreated Creator.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 66 provides a timeless critique of human idolatry:
1. **The Modern Pantheons of Ideology:** Modern human beings often create new forms of shirk—deifying state institutions, celebrity billionaires, scientific consensus, or political ideologies, attributing absolute authority and trust to them. Verse 66 reminds us: all conscious creatures, no matter how powerful or intelligent, are merely created beings belonging entirely to God.
2. **Epistemic Integrity in Faith:** Notice that the Qur'an repeatedly condemns *al-Kharṣ* (blind guessing and unverified speculation). Islam demands that faith be grounded in verifiable truth, sound reason, and authentic divine revelation.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:67

> **He it is Who made for you the night, that you might rest therein, and the day to give sight. Truly in that are signs for a people who hear.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 67 transitions from metaphysical theology to the observable ecological and biological miracles of daily planetary mechanics: *"He it is Who made for you the night, that you might rest therein, and the day to give sight. Truly in that are signs for a people who hear"* (*Huwa alladhī jaʿala lakumu al-layla li-taskunū fīhi wa'l-nahāra mubṣiran inna fī dhālika la-āyātin li-qawmin yasmaʿūn*).

The Qur'an routinely grounds its theological arguments in everyday natural phenomena that humanity takes for granted. The alternation of night and day is not an accidental biological backdrop; it is a meticulously calibrated, intentional divine engineering feat designed specifically to sustain human physiological, psychological, and economic life.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: Circadian Biology and Ecological Balance**

The linguistic phrasing captures the dual functions of planetary rotation:
1. **The Night of Restoration and Stillness (*Li-Taskunū Fīh*):**
   - The root *s-k-n* denotes tranquility, rest, stillness, and emotional recuperation.
   - God blankets the earth in darkness and lowers ambient temperatures so that the biological systems of humans and animals can regenerate, cellular repair can take place, melanin and growth hormones can be secreted, and the mind can recover from the sensory overload of the day.
2. **The Day of Sight and Economic Productivity (*Wa'l-Nahāra Mubṣirā*):**
   - The day is described as *mubṣir* (literally "giving sight" / "illuminating")—a stunning personification where the day itself is described as seeing, because its solar illumination enables human vision, agricultural photosynthesis, trade, industrial productivity, and societal movement.
3. **The Attentive Ear of Reflection (*Li-Qawmin Yasmaʿūn*):**
   - The verse closes with *yasmaʿūn* ("for a people who hear").
   - "Hearing" here refers to conscious, attentive listening (*Samʿ al-Fahm wa'l-Qabūl*)—the willingness to listen to divine revelation with an open heart and connect the physical signs in the cosmos to the wisdom, mercy, and sovereignty of the One Creator.

**Classical Exegesis & The Calibrated Biosphere**

Classical commentators, including Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī and Ibn Kathīr, invite the reader to conduct a profound thought experiment:
- Had the earth been locked in perpetual day (tidally locked facing the sun), biological life would collapse from chronic heat exhaustion, sleep deprivation, and agricultural desiccation.
- Had the earth been locked in perpetual night, agricultural photosynthesis would cease, temperatures would plunge to absolute freezing, and life would freeze into extinction.
- The precise 24-hour planetary rotation that alternates soothing darkness with illuminating light is a direct manifestation of divine love and wisdom (*Raḥmah*).

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 67 provides a vital critique of modern lifestyle dysfunctions:
1. **The Crisis of the Modern 24/7 Society:** Modern technological society, with artificial blue-light screens, 24-hour shift work, and non-stop consumerism, has declared war on the divine design of the night (*li-taskunū fīh*). The result is an epidemic of insomnia, chronic fatigue, metabolic disorders, and depression. Verse 67 calls humanity to respect the natural, God-given circadian rhythms of the body and mind.
2. **A Daily Cycle of Gratitude:** The believer begins the morning thanking God for the light of the day (*al-Nahār*), spends the day in productive, honest labor, and enters the evening seeking peace, family connection, and night worship (*Qiyām al-Layl*).

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:68

> **They say, “God has taken a son.” Glory be to Him! He is the Self-Sufficient. Unto Him belongs whatsoever is in the heavens and whatsoever is in the earth. You have no authority for this! Do you say about God that which you know not?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 68 confronts one of the gravest, most persistent theological transgressions across human religious history: the attribution of offspring to the Divine: *"They say, 'God has taken a son.' Glory be to Him! He is the Self-Sufficient. Unto Him belongs whatsoever is in the heavens and whatsoever is in the earth. You have no authority for this! Do you say about God that which you know not?"* (*Qālū ittakhadha Allāhu waladan subḥānahu huwa al-ghaniyyu lahu mā fī al-samāwāti wa-mā fī al-arḍi in ʿindakum min sulṭānin bi-hādhā a-taqūlūna ʿalā Allāhi mā lā taʿlamūn*).

This critique encompasses multiple historical deviations:
- The pagan Arabs who claimed that the angels were the daughters of God (*Banāt Allāh*).
- Sects among the Christians who deified Jesus as the literal, begotten Son of God.
- Sects among ancient groups who claimed Ezra (*ʿUzayr*) was the son of God.

Verse 68 unleashes an immediate, multi-layered philosophical and theological refutation grounded in the very nature of absolute divinity.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Fourfold Theological Refutation**

The linguistic architecture delivers four crushing arguments:
1. **Subḥānah ("Glory be to Him!"):**
   - Absolute transcendence (*Tanzīh*) and purification of the Divine Essence from every concept of limitation, physical reproduction, anthropomorphism, or deficiency.
2. **Huwa al-Ghaniyy ("He is the Self-Sufficient"):**
   - *Al-Ghaniyy* is the One Who is completely free of all needs, while all creation is entirely dependent upon Him.
   - Why do mortal creatures have children?
     - To preserve genetic lineage against the inevitability of death;
     - To seek assistance, defense, and economic support in old age and physical weakness;
     - To find emotional comfort in loneliness.
   - God Almighty is the Ever-Living (*al-Ḥayy*), the Eternal (*al-Qayyūm*), Who possesses no mortality, no weakness, no loneliness, and no need for assistance. Ascribing a child to God is an ontological contradiction of the definition of divinity.
3. **Universal Dominion (*Lahu mā fī al-samāwāti wa-mā fī al-arḍ*):**
   - Every single entity in the cosmos is already His created, subservient property.
   - A father-son relationship implies shared species, ontological equality, and physical kinship. Between the Uncreated Creator and created matter, there exists an infinite, unbridgeable ontological chasm. All beings are His servants, not His offspring!
4. **Total Lack of Epistemic Authority (*In ʿIndakum min Sulṭānin bi-Hādhā*):**
   - The term *sulṭān* in Qur'anic epistemology signifies clear, authoritative, verifiable proof from divine revelation or sound rational deduction.
   - Making sweeping theological claims about the nature of God without divine revelation is the height of epistemic arrogance (*a-taqūlūna ʿalā Allāhi mā lā taʿlamūn*).

**Classical Exegesis & The Sanctity of Divine Monotheism (Tawḥīd)**

Classical scholars and theologians, including al-Ṭabarī, Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, and Ibn Kathīr, emphasize that this verse aligns with the absolute declaration of Sūrah al-Ikhlāṣ: *"He neither begets nor is born, and there is none comparable unto Him"* (112:3–4).

Imam al-Bukhārī records the *Ḥadīth Qudsī* wherein God Almighty declares the cosmic gravity of this blasphemy:

> *"The son of Adam denies Me, and he has no right to do so; and he insults Me, and he has no right to do so. As for his insulting Me, it is his saying: 'God has taken a son,' whereas I am the One, the Eternal Refuge, Who neither begets nor is born, and there is none comparable unto Me!"* (Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī 4974, from Abū Hurayrah)

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 68 provides essential clarity for interfaith dialogue and philosophical theology:
1. **Preserving the Absolute Transcendence of God:** Anthropomorphism—projecting human biological and psychological needs onto the Divine—is the root of all theological corruption. Islam preserves the pure, pristine transcendence of the Creator: God is not a mortal man, nor does He possess family trees.
2. **The Ethics of Sacred Knowledge:** Verse 68 issues a permanent warning against inventing religious doctrines based on emotional intuition or cultural mythology. In matters concerning the Divine Nature, speak only with verified knowledge (*Sulṭān*).

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:69

> **Say, “Truly those who fabricate lies against God will not prosper.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 69 establishes an immutable, universal law of spiritual and historical cause and effect: *"Say, 'Truly those who fabricate lies against God will not prosper'"* (*Qul inna alladhīna yaftarūna ʿalā Allāhi al-kadhiba lā yufliḥūn*).

Building directly upon the condemnation of theological fabrications in verse 68—where the polytheists and deviant sects falsely claimed that God has taken a son or daughters—verse 69 commands the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ to deliver a definitive, unshakeable verdict on the ultimate outcome of all religious fraud, theological distortion, and spiritual deceit.

The verse addresses every individual, institution, and religious hierarchy throughout human history that speaks on behalf of the Divine without revelation, alters sacred scripture, or invents doctrines to control human beings: their temporary worldly success is a hollow illusion that terminates in absolute spiritual bankruptcy.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The True Meaning of Falāḥ**

The linguistic phrasing establishes an absolute negative verdict stamped with certainty:
- **The Emphatic Nominal Sentence (*Inna Alladhīna Yaftarūn...*):** The particle *inna* confirms the universal, unyielding nature of the law across all historical eras.
- **The Act of *Iftirāʾ* (Fabrication):** Derived from the root *f-r-y*, *al-Iftirāʾ* denotes deliberately cutting, carving, or inventing a malicious falsehood from pure imagination. To invent lies about God's nature, laws, or commandments is the highest possible crime against reality.
- **The Comprehensive Negation of *Falāḥ* (*Lā Yufliḥūn*):**
  - In classical Arabic and Islamic theology, *Falāḥ* is not merely transient financial accumulation, political power, or media celebrity.
  - *Falāḥ* represents ultimate, comprehensive, and eternal flourishing: peace of heart in this life, purity of soul, safety at the moment of death, acquittal on the Day of Judgment, and eternal elevation in Paradise.
  - The absolute negation *lā yufliḥūn* ("they will never prosper") proclaims that even if a religious charlatan or corrupt tyrant amasses billions of dollars and dominates global headlines, their success is a fragile mirage that collapses into eternal ruin at the threshold of the grave.

**Classical Exegesis & The Historical Fate of Religious Deceivers**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and al-Qurṭubī, unpack the historical proof of this verse:
- Throughout human history, false prophets (such as Musaylimah the Liar), corrupt religious hierarchies that sold indulgences, and totalitarian regimes that claimed divine mandates all ended in historical ignominy, humiliation, and destruction.
- Falsehood contains the seeds of its own internal decay; only truth (*al-Ḥaqq*) possesses enduring ontological permanence.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 69 provides a vital warning and reassurance for modern life:
1. **Reassurance in the Face of Corrupt Success:** Sincere believers often look upon dishonest corporate leaders, corrupt politicians, and deceptive media figures who amass vast fortunes and ask in confusion: *"Why are they succeeding?"* Verse 69 delivers the divine verdict: their success is an illusion (*lā yufliḥūn*). True success is measured by the eternal ledger of the soul, not by temporal bank accounts.
2. **The Imperative of Truthfulness in Religious Speech:** The verse warns every individual to maintain absolute honesty in their religious practice and discourse. Never twist sacred texts to justify personal desires, seek worldly popularity, or appease cultural trends.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:70

> **[There is] enjoyment in this world, then unto Us is their return, and then We shall make them taste the severe punishment for having disbelieved.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 70 places the apparent worldly prosperity of the wicked in its true, fleeting temporal perspective: *"[There is] enjoyment in this world, then unto Us is their return, and then We shall make them taste the severe punishment for having disbelieved"* (*Matāʿun fī al-dunyā thumma ilaynā marjiʿuhum thumma nudhīquhumu al-ʿadhāba al-shadīda bimā kānū yakfurūn*).

In verse 69, the Qur'an declared that those who lie against God will not prosper; here, verse 70 addresses the natural observation that such individuals often live in luxurious mansions, enjoy fine dining, and wield immense political power on earth. The divine discourse contextualizes this earthly luxury as mere *Matāʿ*—a brief, passing convenience that lasts only for a fleeting heartbeat before the arrival of eternal accounting.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Three Chronological Stages**

The linguistic architecture of the verse unfolds across three sequential chronological stages:

1. **The Fleeting Luxury (*Matāʿun fī al-Dunyā*):**
   - The noun *matāʿ* in classical Arabic denotes a small, temporary provision—like a cheap utensil or towel utilized during a road trip and soon discarded.
   - The Qur'an never denies that disbelievers and tyrants can enjoy worldly luxuries; rather, it emphasizes that in the cosmic scale of eternity, seventy years of worldly pleasure is less than a microsecond compared to everlasting existence.
