   - God commanded Moses to lead the Israelites out of Egypt by night. Trapped at the shores of the Red Sea with imperial chariots approaching behind, Moses struck the sea with his staff: the waters parted into twelve dry paths with walls of water towering like colossal mountains, allowing the entire nation to cross safely.
2. **The Arrogant Pursuit into the Abyss (*Baghyan wa-ʿAdwā*):**
   - *Baghy*: Tyrannical oppression and bloodlust.
   - *ʿAdw*: Hostility and aggressive transgression.
   - Witnessing this cosmic miracle, rational minds would have stopped in awe. But Pharaoh, blinded by boundless arrogance, ordered his entire armored army into the parted seabed to slaughter the Israelites.
3. **The Moment of Drowning and the Deathbed Conversion (*Ḥattā Idhā Adrakahu al-Gharaq...*):**
   - The verb *adraka* denotes overtaking, capturing, and enclosing.
   - As the towering walls of seawater collapsed upon him, choking his lungs and dragging his gold armor into the abyss, Pharaoh was stripped of every shred of pride. In ultimate terror, he screamed a three-part confession of faith:
     1. *"I believe!"* (*Āmantu*)
     2. *"There is no god save Him in Whom the Children of Israel believe!"* (*Lā ilāha illā alladhī āmanat bihi banū Isrāʾīl*)
     3. *"And I am of those who submit (Muslimūn)!"* (*Wa-anā mina al-muslimīn*)

**Classical Exegesis & The Psychology of Pharaoh's Desperate Cry**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and al-Qurṭubī, unpack the profound irony of Pharaoh's confession:
- The man who had proclaimed to his people: *"I know not that you have any god other than me!"* (28:38) and *"I am your lord most high!"* (79:24),
- Was reduced to defining God through the faith of the very Hebrew slaves he had whipped and oppressed: *"in Whom the Children of Israel believe!"*
- His pride completely disintegrated, but as the next verse reveals, his forced conversion arrived too late.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 90 provides the ultimate historical lesson on the fate of tyrants:
1. **The Inevitable Collapse of Arrogance:** Every tyrant in human history eventually reaches their Red Sea. Worldly power, military weapons, and propaganda systems function only within the boundaries permitted by God; the moment the divine decree matures, the very elements of nature turn to consume the oppressor.
2. **The Timing of True Faith:** Faith is a gift to be embraced today, during the sunshine of life. Waiting until the waters of death are choking your throat leads only to the eternal tragedy of Pharaoh.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:91

> **“Now? When thou didst disobey before, and wast among the corrupters?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 91 delivers the decisive, cutting divine response to Pharaoh's drowning declaration of faith recorded in verse 90: *"Now? When thou didst disobey before, and wast among the corrupters?"* (*Āl-āna wa-qad ʿaṣayta qablu wa-kunta mina al-mufsidīn*).

The divine verdict cuts through the roaring waters with absolute finality. This verse stands as the supreme theological precedent in Islamic creed regarding the rejection of forced, deathbed repentance (*Tawbat al-Yaʾs* / *Īmān al-Iḍṭirār*).

Pharaoh spent decades slaughtering innocent infant boys, enslaving millions of human beings, declaring himself to be the supreme deity of the universe, and mocking every single prophetic sign sent to his palace. His proclamation of faith at the moment of drowning was not an act of moral repentance; it was merely an involuntary reaction to impending doom, and was therefore rejected with absolute divine justice.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Devastating Irony of Āl-Ān**

The linguistic phrasing carries supreme rhetorical and legal force:
- **The Thunderclap of *Āl-Ān* (Now?!):** The interrogative particle *āl-āna* ("Now?!") is loaded with supreme divine irony, reprimand, and finality.
  - "Now, when your physical strength is broken, your golden chariots are at the bottom of the sea, and the Angel of Death is standing over you, you claim faith?!"
- **The Record of Past Transgression (*Wa-qad ʿAṣayta Qabl*):** The past perfect clause *wa-qad ʿaṣayta qabl* reminds the tyrant of his extensive criminal record: a lifetime of deliberate, calculated moral rebellion and defiance of divine law during the period when he possessed free will.
- **The Category of *Al-Mufsidīn* (The Corrupters):** The root *f-s-d* defines Pharaoh's civilizational legacy: *Fasād*—systemic corruption, state terror, social degradation, and the violent suppression of monotheism.

**Classical Exegesis & The Archangel Gabriel's Intervention**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and al-Qurṭubī, cite the authentic, remarkable transmission recorded in *Jāmiʿ al-Tirmidhī* and *Musnad Aḥmad*:

> The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ recounted: *"Gabriel ﷺ said to me: 'O Muhammad! If only you could have seen me when I was taking the dark mud from the bottom of the sea and stuffing it into Pharaoh's mouth, out of fear that the divine mercy might reach him!'"* (Jāmiʿ al-Tirmidhī 3107; Musnad Aḥmad 2144, graded ṣaḥīḥ)

Gabriel's action was not a denial of divine mercy, but a reflection of the angels' intense anger toward the most arrogant tyrant in human history, knowing that Pharaoh's repentance was completely fraudulent.

**The Theological Doctrine of the Boundary of Repentance (Al-Ghargharah)**

This verse establishes the universal theological law articulated by the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ:

> *"Verily, God accepts the repentance of a servant as long as his soul has not reached the death rattle (mā lam yugharghir)."* (Jāmiʿ al-Tirmidhī 3537; Sunan Ibn Mājah 4253, graded ḥasan)

As long as a person breathes in the realm of testing, the door of repentance (*Tawbah*) is wide open; but the moment the soul reaches the throat and physical sight of the next world is unveiled, the examination period is permanently closed.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 91 provides an urgent warning against spiritual procrastination:
1. **The Fallacy of the Deathbed Conversion:** Many people live hedonistic, unethical lives, convincing themselves: *"I will repent on my deathbed."* Verse 91 dismantles this delusion: you cannot outsmart the Creator. A lifetime of arrogant choices cannot be erased by a panicked, coerced confession when destruction strikes.
2. **The Urgency of Sincere Repentance Today:** Turn to God today, while you possess health, free will, and the capacity to rectify your deeds.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:92

> **So this day We shall save thee in thy body, that thou mayest be a sign for those who come after thee. And truly many among human beings are heedless of Our signs.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 92 unveils an astonishing historical and prophetic decree concerning the physical remains of Pharaoh: *"So this day We shall save thee in thy body, that thou mayest be a sign for those who come after thee. And truly many among human beings are heedless of Our signs"* (*Fa'l-yawma nunajjīka bi-badanika li-takūna li-man khalfaka āyah wa-inna kathīran mina al-nāsi ʿan āyātinā la-ghāfilūn*).

When the Red Sea closed over Pharaoh and his imperial hosts, a profound psychological crisis arose among the traumatized Israelites. Centuries of psychological conditioning under Egyptian tyranny made it difficult for some Israelites to believe that the seemingly immortal god-king of Egypt was truly dead. They whispered in fear: *"Pharaoh cannot die; he has merely retreated to his palaces!"*

To shatter this myth of imperial divinity forever, God issued a specific command: Pharaoh's physical body would be preserved and cast upon the shore for all to witness.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Preservation of the Physical Shell**

The linguistic phrasing contains exquisite precision:
- **The Irony of Salvation (*Nunajjīka bi-Badanik*):**
  - In verse 90, Pharaoh screamed for salvation (*Najāt*). God responds with profound irony: "We shall save you—not your soul from damnation, but your lifeless physical corpse (*bi-badanik*)!"
  - The word *badan* refers specifically to the physical, soulless trunk, shell, and armor of the body.
- **The Purpose of the Preservation (*Li-Takūna li-man Khalfaka Āyah*):**
  - *Li-man khalfak*: For those who come after you—both the contemporary Israelites standing on the shore and all subsequent human generations across history.
  - *Āyah*: A living, visible, physical sign of the total collapse of human arrogance before divine sovereignty.
- **The Tragedy of Heedlessness (*Wa-inna Kathīran mina al-Nāsi ʿan Āyātinā la-Ghāfilūn*):**
  - The verse concludes with a sobering reflection: despite the preservation of these historical signs, the vast majority of human beings walk past them in deep spiritual blindness (*Ghaflah*).

**Modern Archaeology & The Prophetic Miracle of the Preserved Pharaoh**

In modern times, this verse has revealed an extraordinary, multi-millennial evidential dimension:
- In ancient Egypt, the bodies of pharaohs were mummified and entombed. Centuries later, modern archaeology discovered the mummified bodies of the New Kingdom pharaohs (such as Ramesses II and his successor Merneptah, historically associated with the Exodus narrative).
- Forensic examinations of the mummy of Merneptah revealed extensive salt deposits and trauma consistent with drowning in the sea and rapid extraction.
- Today, the physical body of the Pharaoh lies in a glass display case in the National Museum of Egyptian Civilization in Cairo, viewed by millions of tourists, historians, and scientists from every corner of the globe.
- Revealed in the 7th-century Arabian desert to an unlettered Prophet ﷺ, the Qur'an explicitly prophesied that Pharaoh's **physical body** (*badan*) would be uniquely preserved across historical eras to serve as an enduring sign (*Āyah*) for all future generations.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 92 provides a profound meditation on the ultimate destination of worldly power:
1. **The Ultimate Humiliation of the Tyrant:** The man who declared *"I am your lord most high"* now lies as a dried, shriveled, lifeless mummy behind a glass display case, ticketed for public curiosity. Worldly power without God ends in absolute physical and spiritual humiliation.
2. **Reflecting Upon the Signs of History:** Millions walk past Pharaoh's mummy in museums, take photographs, and read archaeological plaques, yet remain completely oblivious (*ghāfilūn*) to the monumental spiritual lesson staring them in the face: that human arrogance is completely powerless before the Lord of the worlds.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:93

> **And We settled the Children of Israel in an agreeable settlement and provided them with good things. Then they did not differ until knowledge had come unto them. Truly thy Lord will judge between them on the Day of Resurrection concerning that wherein they used to differ.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 93 traces the historical sequel of the Exodus: the settlement of the Children of Israel in the Holy Land, the divine showering of pure provisions, and the tragic emergence of sectarian division and theological disputes: *"And We settled the Children of Israel in an agreeable settlement and provided them with good things. Then they did not differ until knowledge had come unto them. Truly thy Lord will judge between them on the Day of Resurrection concerning that wherein they used to differ"* (*Wa-la-qad bawwaʾnā banī Isrāʾīla mubawwaʾa ṣidqin wa-razaqnāhum mina al-ṭayyibāti fa-mā ikhtalafū ḥattā jāʾahumu al-ʿilmu inna Rabbaka yaqḍī baynahum yawma al-qiyāmati fīmā kānū fīhi yakhtalifūn*).

Following their miraculous liberation from Egyptian slavery, God fulfilled His divine promise to the Israelites: granting them an honorable, fertile homeland and showering them with pure, lawful sustenance (*al-Ṭayyibāt*).

However, the verse highlights a tragic recurring pathology in religious history: sectarian fracturing occurred not due to a lack of knowledge, but **after knowledge had arrived**, driven by spiritual envy, personal egos, and political ambition.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Land of Dignity (Mubawwaʾa Ṣidq)**

The linguistic architecture of the verse provides profound historical and sociological depth:
- **The Honorable Dwelling Place (*Mubawwaʾa Ṣidq*):**
  - The term *mubawwaʾ* denotes a secure, settled, and established dwelling place.
  - Adding the noun *ṣidq* (truth, honor, excellence) indicates an honorable, fertile, and blessed territory—identified by classical exegetes, such as al-Ṭabarī and Ibn Kathīr, as the blessed Levant (*al-Shām*), the fertile lands of Palestine, and parts of Egypt following the collapse of Pharaoh's dynasty.
- **The Showering of Provisions (*Mina al-Ṭayyibāt*):**
  - God blessed them with pure, wholesome agricultural abundance, manna, quails, fresh water springs, peace, and security.
- **The Paradox of Division After Knowledge (*Fa-mā Ikhtalafū Ḥattā Jāʾahumu al-ʿIlm*):**
  - The particle *ḥattā* denotes the tragic turning point: they did not splinter into hostile sects because they lacked guidance; they possessed the Torah, prophetic leadership, and direct knowledge (*al-ʿIlm*).
  - Sectarian division was birthed by spiritual envy (*baghyan baynahum*), lust for religious prestige, competitive tribalism, and personal ego. They weaponized sacred texts to serve partisan interests.

**Classical Exegesis & The Eschatological Resolution of All Disputes**

The verse concludes: *"Truly thy Lord will judge between them on the Day of Resurrection concerning that wherein they used to differ."*
- Worldly theological polemics and partisan disputes will be conclusively resolved in the divine court.
- God will expose who sincerely sought His pleasure and who manipulated sacred texts for worldly power and prestige.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 93 provides an urgent warning for the Muslim Ummah and all religious communities:
1. **The Danger of Religious Sectarianism:** Ignorance is dangerous, but **knowledge corrupted by ego and arrogance** is far more destructive. When religious scholars and leaders allow envy (*ḥasad*) and tribalism to dictate their discourse, they splinter the community into hostile factions. Verse 93 warns the followers of the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ not to repeat the tragic sectarian mistakes of previous faith communities.
2. **The Ethics of Sacred Knowledge:** Sacred knowledge was revealed to unite hearts in the worship of God, establish social justice, and cultivate humility. When knowledge is used to fuel arrogance, debate, and division, it becomes a curse upon its possessor.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:94

> **So if thou art in doubt concerning that which We have sent down unto thee, ask those who have been reading the Book before thee. The truth has indeed come unto thee from thy Lord, so be thou not of the waverers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 94 utilizes a striking, classical rhetorical mode of address: *"So if thou art in doubt concerning that which We have sent down unto thee, ask those who have been reading the Book before thee. The truth has indeed come unto thee from thy Lord, so be thou not of the waverers"* (*Fa-in kunta fī shakkin mimmā anzalnā ilayka fa'sʾal alladhīna yaqraʾūna al-kitāba min qablika la-qad jāʾaka al-ḥaqqu min Rabbika fa-lā takūnanna mina al-mumtarīn*).

Classical exegetes, including Ibn ʿAbbās, Qatādah, al-Ḥasan al-Baṣrī, al-Ṭabarī, and Ibn Kathīr, clarify the essential rhetorical and theological nature of this address:
- In classical Arabic rhetoric, a conditional address directed at the leader of a community (*Fa-in kunta...*) is often intended pedagogically for his **followers, skeptics, and the general public**, rather than implying that the Prophet himself experienced personal doubt (*Iyyāki aʿnī wa'smaʿī yā Jārah*).
- The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ possessed the highest degree of certainty (*Yaqīn*) and never doubted his divine mission for a single heartbeat.
- In fact, upon the revelation of this verse, the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ famously proclaimed:
  > *"I do not doubt, and I do not ask!"* (Tafsīr al-Ṭabarī, Musnad ʿAbd ibn Ḥumayd)

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: Corroboration of Previous Scriptures**

The linguistic architecture of the verse provides profound evidential depth:
- **The Historical Corroboration (*Fa'sʾal alladhīna Yaqraʾūna al-Kitāba min Qablik*):**
  - If the pagan Makkans or skeptical listeners harbored doubts about the truth of these ancient prophetic events (such as the narratives of Noah, Moses, and Pharaoh), they could consult the learned scholars of previous scriptures (such as ʿAbdullāh ibn Salām, Waraqah ibn Nawfal, and Salmān al-Fārisī).
  - These scholars would confirm that the Qur'an accurately narrates prophetic history and matches the core monotheistic prophecies of previous revelations.
- **The Uncompromised Affirmation of Truth (*La-qad Jāʾaka al-Ḥaqqu min Rabbik*):**
  - The combination of the oath particle *lām* and the confirmatory *qad* affirms that the Qur'an is absolute, undeniable truth descending directly from the Lord of the worlds.
- **The Prohibition of Wavering (*Fa-lā Takūnanna mina al-Mumtarīn*):**
  - The root *m-r-y* in *al-mumtarīn* denotes wavering, entertaining unwarranted skepticism, or being swayed by sophistry. Truth has been established with crystal-clear evidence; therefore, believers must stand with firm, unshakeable certitude.

**Classical Exegesis & The Unity of Divine Revelation**

Classical scholars emphasize that this verse highlights the harmonious continuity of divine revelation:
- The Qur'an does not emerge in a historical vacuum; it is the final, crowning chapter in a long, unbroken lineage of divine guidance.
- The learned, sincere scholars of the Torah and Gospel recognized the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ through the descriptions preserved in their sacred texts.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 94 provides an essential foundation for intellectual certainty in faith:
1. **The Inevitability of Questions vs. Toxic Wavering:** Islam encourages honest questioning and intellectual investigation, but warns against *al-Mirtyah*—cynical, chronic wavering that refuses to accept truth even after decisive proof has been presented.
2. **The Evidential Power of Comparative Scriptural Studies:** The remarkable historical and theological coherence between the Qur'an and the authentic remnants of previous scriptures stands as a powerful empirical testament to their shared divine origin.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:95

> **And be not thou of those who deny the signs of God, lest thou be among the losers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 95 continues the emphatic, uncompromising warning against the rejection of divine signs: *"And be not thou of those who deny the signs of God, lest thou be among the losers"* (*Wa-lā takūnanna mina alladhīna kadhdhabū bi-āyāti Allāhi fa-takūna mina al-khāsirīn*).

Like verse 94, this severe prohibition is addressed grammatically to the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ as the representative and exemplar of all humanity, but its actual target is every individual soul hearing the Qur'anic message:
- Denying the signs of God (*Takdhīb bi-Āyāt Allāh*) is the ultimate, unrivaled spiritual catastrophe.
- "Signs" (*Āyāt*) encompass both the revealed scriptural verses of the Qur'an and the observable cosmic signatures of divine design in nature.
- When an individual systematically ignores, mocks, or denies these signs out of arrogance or worldly attachment, they cut themselves off from the sole source of eternal life and guidance.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Emphatic Prohibition**

The linguistic architecture carries supreme grammatical intensity:
- **The Heavy Emphatic Nūn (*Lā Takūnanna*):** The prohibitive *lā* coupled with the energetic *nūn al-tawkīd al-thaqīlah* forms the strongest possible prohibition in the Arabic language: "Do not under any conceivable circumstance be among those who deny the signs of God!"
- **The Inevitable Outcome: Total Existential Bankruptcy (*Fa-takūna mina al-Khāsirīn*):**
  - The causal *fāʾ* (*fāʾ al-sababiyyah*) leads directly into the catastrophic consequence: *al-Khusrān*.
  - The term *al-khāsirūn* signifies those who have suffered total, irreversible bankruptcy. In the commerce of existence, the greatest loss is trading eternal bliss in the Garden of Peace for a fleeting moment of worldly status, arriving at the Day of Judgment completely empty-handed.
- **The Category of Takdhīb (Denial):** The root *k-dh-b* denotes lying, denying, and treating truth as false. Denial of divine signs is an active moral violation against reality.

**Classical Exegesis & The Pedagogical Purpose of Prophetic Address**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, explain why such warnings are addressed to the infallible Prophet ﷺ:
- If the greatest, most beloved of all creation—the Messenger of God ﷺ—is addressed with such severe warnings against denying divine signs, how much more vigilant, humble, and reverent must ordinary human beings be!
- It establishes that in the court of divine justice, there is zero favoritism: anyone who rejects divine signs inevitably falls into total ruin (*al-Khusrān*).
- It teaches every believer to guard their faith with continuous vigilance, never becoming arrogant or complacent about their spiritual state.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 95 provides an urgent warning for modern believers:
1. **The Danger of Cynicism:** Modern secular culture promotes cynicism, irony, and skepticism as signs of intellectual sophistication. Verse 95 exposes this as a fatal spiritual trap: cynical denial of sacred truth leads only to spiritual death and existential loss.
2. **Cherishing the Signs of God:** The believer approaches the signs of God—the verses of the Qur'an, the beauty of the cosmos, the blessings of daily life—with deep reverence, gratitude, and active faith, safeguarding their soul from eternal loss.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:96

> **Truly those against whom the Word of thy Lord has come due will not believe,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 96 reveals a profound metaphysical and psychological reality governing human hearts: *"Truly those against whom the Word of thy Lord has come due will not believe"* (*Inna alladhīna ḥaqqat ʿalayhim kalimatu Rabbika lā yuʾminūn*).

This verse closely parallels verse 33 of this Sūrah, explaining the spiritual mechanics behind persistent, incurable disbelief. It provides immense emotional and spiritual solace to the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ: his mission was not a failure because chieftains like Abū Jahl, Abū Lahab, and Pharaoh rejected his message; rather, their hearts had already withered beyond spiritual redemption.

The verse addresses the classical theological balance between divine pre-measurement (*al-Qadar*), divine omniscience, and human moral responsibility.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Realization of the Word (Ḥaqqat Kalimah)**

The linguistic phrasing provides profound theological precision:
- **The Absolute Affirmation (*Inna Alladhīna...*):** The particle *inna* confirms the unshakeable certainty of the law across all epochs.
- **The Binding Decree (*Ḥaqqat ʿAlayhim Kalimatu Rabbik*):**
  - The verb *ḥaqqat* denotes becoming binding, confirmed, due, and irreversible.
  - The "Word of thy Lord" (*kalimatu Rabbik*) refers to God's eternal, infallible foreknowledge and just decree that individuals who repeatedly, willfully extinguish their conscience, mock the prophets, and wage war against truth will reach a point of irreversible moral calcification.
- **The Inevitable Outcome (*Lā Yuʾminūn*):** Their spiritual faculties have become so atrophied through persistent sin that they will not believe.

**Classical Exegesis & The Justice of Divine Decrees**

Classical theologians, including al-Ṭabarī, Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, and Ibn Kathīr, clarify that God never forces a soul into disbelief against its will:
- Sealing a heart is the **retributive consequence** of the person's own persistent, chosen rebellion.
- When an individual systematically shuts every door of guidance, mocks every warning, and chooses arrogance, God confirms them upon the darkness they chose.
- As God declares in Sūrah al-Baqarah: *"God has set a seal upon their hearts and upon their hearing, and over their vision is a veil"* (2:7)—this seal is the consequence of their own prior denial.
- Divine justice is absolute: God simply leaves the rebel to the darkness they loved and pursued.

**The Pastoral Comfort for the Messenger ﷺ**

The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ grieved deeply over those who rejected the message, almost destroying himself with sorrow over their impending doom (as described in Qur'an 18:6). Verse 96 consoles him:
- Do not exhaust your soul in grief over those who refuse to believe.
- The failure is in their hardened hearts, not in your conveyance of the message.
- Your sole duty is *al-Balāgh* (clear conveyance), while the hearts belong to God.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 96 delivers a sobering warning against taking faith for granted:
1. **The Fragility of the Spiritual Heart:** The human heart is sensitive: continuous moral compromises, arrogance, and mocking sacred values gradually harden the heart until it loses the capacity to feel awe or accept truth.
2. **Humility in Calling Others to Islam:** When sharing faith, the believer must remember that guidance is in the hand of God alone. We deliver the message with excellence, leaving the hearts to the Creator.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:97

> **even if every sign should come unto them, till they see the painful punishment.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Following verse 96, verse 97 explains the extreme depth and tenacity of spiritual calcification: *"even if every sign should come unto them, till they see the painful punishment"* (*Wa-law jāʾathum kullu āyatin ḥattā yarawu al-ʿadhāba al-alīm*).

This verse completely dismantles the common secular illusion that skepticism and atheism are caused merely by a lack of empirical evidence:
- If every conceivable cosmic miracle were performed before their eyes—if mountains moved, the dead rose from their graves, the moon split in two, and angels descended visibly—an arrogant, hardened heart would still dismiss it as mass hallucination, optical illusion, or advanced technology.
- Spiritual blindness is an affliction of the **will and the heart**, not of the intellect.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Total Inefficacy of Signs**

The linguistic architecture emphasizes the totality of the signs:
- **The Universal Quantifier (*Kullu Āyah*):** The phrase *kullu āyatin* ("every single sign") encompasses all categories of evidence—rational proofs, linguistic miracles, cosmic ecological marvels, and prophetic signs.
- **The Boundary of Forced Realization (*Ḥattā Yarawu al-ʿAdhāba al-Alīm*):**
  - The particle *ḥattā* marks the only moment when their denial finally shatters: when they physically see the painful punishment (*al-ʿadhāb al-alīm*).
  - Just like Pharaoh who maintained his arrogance through nine catastrophic plagues only to scream belief when drowning in the sea, hardened deniers only surrender when physical destruction is visibly before them. But as established throughout this Sūrah, belief coerced by impending doom is legally and spiritually invalid.

**Classical Exegesis & The Historical Proof of Obstinacy**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī and Ibn Kathīr, highlight historical examples:
- The people of Thamūd demanded a specific sign: a giant pregnant she-camel emerging from solid rock. God granted them that exact miracle, yet they still hamstrung the camel and mocked the prophet!
- Pharaoh witnessed nine miraculous plagues, yet persisted in arrogance until he drowned.
- The Makkan polytheists witnessed the splitting of the moon (*Inshiqāq al-Qamar*), yet said: *"This is continuous sorcery!"* (54:2).

**The Psychology of Ideological Hardening**

When a human being decides that their ego, lifestyle, or political power is more important than truth, no evidence in the universe can convince them:
- Cognitive dissonance causes the ego to construct elaborate defense mechanisms to preserve its pride.
- They will invent endless rationalizations, conspiracy theories, and cynical excuses to avoid moral surrender.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 97 provides profound insight into the psychology of modern skepticism:
1. **The Limits of Empirical Evidence:** Modern skeptics often say: *"If God just performed a miracle for me right now, I would believe."* Verse 97 reveals that skepticism is rarely an intellectual problem; it is almost always a moral and psychological problem. If a heart is committed to its own ego and desires, no amount of evidence will ever suffice.
2. **Purifying the Sincerity of the Heart:** The seeker of truth must purify their intention: approach reality with an open, humble heart, ready to submit to truth wherever it is found.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:98

> **Why was there not a town that believed and benefited from its belief, except the people of Jonah? When they believed, We removed from them the punishment of disgrace in the life of this world and granted them enjoyment for a time.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 98 highlights the extraordinary, unprecedented historical exception in the annals of prophetic history: the remarkable collective repentance of the People of Prophet Jonah (Qawm Yūnus, peace be upon him): *"Why was there not a town that believed and benefited from its belief, except the people of Jonah? When they believed, We removed from them the punishment of disgrace in the life of this world and granted them enjoyment for a time"* (*Fa-lawlā kānat qaryatun āmanat fa-nafaʿahā īmānuhā illā qawma Yūnus lammā āmanū kashafnā ʿanhum ʿadhāba al-khizyi fī al-ḥayāti al-dunyā wa-mattaʿnāhum ilā ḥīn*).

Across all human history, every ancient civilization that faced impending divine destruction—the people of Noah, ʿĀd, Thamūd, Sodom, and Pharaoh—persisted in arrogance until the punishment physically struck, at which point their late repentance was rejected.

The people of Jonah (Nineveh, in modern-day Mosul, Iraq) are the **sole exception** in human history: when they saw the signs of impending doom, they initiated collective, sincere repentance before the catastrophe physically fell, and God miraculously lifted the punishment from them.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Singular Exception**

The linguistic architecture carries profound theological and historical depth:
- **The Rhetorical Interrogative of Regret and Exhortation (*Fa-lawlā Kānat Qaryatun...*):**
  - The particle *lawlā* is used here for *al-taḥḍīḍ wa'l-targhīb* (intense incitement and sorrowful questioning): "Why was there not a single town throughout historical epochs that had the wisdom to believe in time so that its faith would benefit it, except the people of Jonah?!"
- **The Exceptional Extraction (*Illā Qawma Yūnus*):**
  - The grammatical exception (*Istithnāʾ*) highlights the unique historical status of Nineveh.
- **The Removal of the Punishment of Disgrace (*Kashafnā ʿAnhum ʿAdhāba al-Khizy*):**
  - The verb *kashafa* denotes lifting, peeling back, or removing a dark covering.
  - God removed *ʿadhāb al-khizy* (the punishment of worldly humiliation and destruction) and granted them peace, security, and prosperity for generations (*wa-mattaʿnāhum ilā ḥīn*).

**Classical Exegesis & The Extraordinary Story of Nineveh's Repentance**

Classical commentators, including Ibn ʿAbbās, Mujāhid, al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and al-Qurṭubī, recount the dramatic scene:
- When Prophet Jonah (Yūnus) warned them that divine punishment would arrive in three days, and departed their city in frustration,
- The people observed ominous black clouds of divine wrath gathering over their city.
- Unlike all other nations, they did not mock or rationalize. Led by their elders and king, the entire population of over one hundred thousand people marched into the desert in sackcloth and ashes.
- They separated mothers from their nursing infants and livestock from their young, fell into prostration, wept, and screamed in sincere, collective repentance to the One True God before the physical destruction descended.
- God accepted their sincere repentance and lifted the impending catastrophe.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 98 provides the ultimate message of hope in the Holy Qur'an:
1. **The Boundless Mercy of God:** No matter how dark the impending crisis, no matter how severe the past sins of a society, sincere, unreserved collective repentance can overturn divine wrath and unlock divine mercy. The door of divine mercy is never closed to those who turn to God before physical destruction strikes.
2. **A Model for National Repentance:** When modern nations face severe crises—pandemics, ecological collapse, economic ruin, or social breakdown—the true path to recovery is moral and spiritual: collective humility, repentance, justice, and returning to the Creator.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:99

> **And had thy Lord willed, all who are on the earth would have believed together. Wilt thou then compel human beings till they become believers?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 99 articulates one of the most foundational philosophical and theological principles of Islamic creed and governance: the reality of free moral agency (*al-Ikhtiyār*) and the absolute prohibition of religious coercion: *"And had thy Lord willed, all who are on the earth would have believed together. Wilt thou then compel human beings till they become believers?"* (*Wa-law shāʾa Rabbuka la-āmana man fī al-arḍi kulluhum jamīʿā a-fa-anta tukrihu al-nāsa ḥattā yakūnū muʾminīn*).

The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ possessed boundless compassion and love for humanity, grieving intensely over those who rejected guidance. In his profound desire for their salvation, he wished that everyone would embrace the light of Islam.

God addresses His Messenger with supreme wisdom: divine governance has intentionally designed the earthly realm not as a realm of forced obedience, but as an arena of moral testing based on human free will.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: Free Will vs. Coercion**

The linguistic architecture establishes the metaphysical framework of human existence:
- **The Hypothetical Omnipotence of God (*Wa-law Shāʾa Rabbuk...*):**
  - God possesses the absolute, infinite power to compel every human being on earth to believe instantaneously:
    - He could have created humanity with the involuntary obedience of the angels, who cannot disobey God.
    - He could have wired the human brain so that disbelief is neurologically impossible.
  - The emphatic combination *kulluhum jamīʿan* ("all of them together, every single one") affirms that universal belief is entirely within God's power.
- **The Divine Design of Free Moral Agency (*al-Ikhtiyār*):**
  - However, in His supreme wisdom, God designed the earthly realm as a testing ground (*Dār al-Ibtilāʾ*). The entire value, beauty, and virtue of faith (*Īmān*) and righteousness lies in the fact that a human being possesses the capacity to disobey, yet freely chooses to humble their ego and love their Creator.
- **The Prohibition of Coercion (*A-fa-anta Tukrihu al-Nās*):**
  - The interrogative *hamzah* expresses absolute rhetorical prohibition: "Will you then force people to become believers?!"
  - This establishes the bedrock Islamic legal and moral principle echoed in Sūrah al-Baqarah: *"There is no compulsion in religion"* (2:256).

**Classical Exegesis & The Illegitimacy of Forced Conversion**

Classical scholars and jurists, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and al-Qurṭubī, emphasize that faith cannot be forced at sword-point:
- Genuine faith requires voluntary intellectual conviction and heartfelt submission (*Taṣdīq bi'l-Qalb*).
- Coercion produces only hypocrisy (*Nifāq*), which is an abomination in the sight of God.
- The duty of the Prophet ﷺ and believers is solely *al-Balāgh*—inviting, explaining, and exemplifying truth with wisdom and beautiful preaching.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 99 provides a timeless charter for religious freedom and peaceful coexistence:
1. **The Islamic Foundation for Religious Freedom:** Long before modern human rights declarations, the Qur'an established that religious conscience cannot be coerced. Islam protected the churches, synagogues, and religious autonomy of non-Muslim citizens within Islamic civilization.
2. **Emotional Peace for the Caller to Islam:** Believers engaged in daʿwah are liberated from anxiety: your role is to deliver the message with love and clarity; whether a person accepts or rejects is between them and their Creator.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:100

> **And it is not for any soul to believe save by the Leave of God. And He places defilement upon those who use not their reason.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 100 articulates the profound metaphysical harmony between divine grace (*Tawfīq*) and human intellectual responsibility (*al-ʿAql*): *"And it is not for any soul to believe save by the Leave of God. And He places defilement upon those who use not their reason"* (*Wa-mā kāna li-nafsin an tuʾmina illā bi-idhni Allāhi wa-yajʿalu al-rijsa ʿalā alladhīna lā yaʿqilūn*).

Building directly upon verse 99, this verse resolves one of the deepest questions in theological philosophy: the relationship between Divine Sovereignty and Human Reason. The verse establishes that faith is a transcendent gift granted by divine permission (*Bi-Idhni Allāh*), while condemning those who abandon their God-given intellect as spiritually defiled.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: Grace and Reason**

The linguistic architecture delivers two profound principles:

1. **The Sovereign Monopoly of Divine Grace (*Bi-Idhni Allāh*):**
   - The construction *mā kāna li-nafsin an... illā* denotes structural dependency: no human soul can attain genuine faith through personal cleverness, debate skills, or intellectual gymnastics alone.
   - Faith (*Īmān*) is a divine light ignited in the heart by the "Leave of God" (*bi-idhni Allāh*)—His grace, facilitation, and divine assistance (*Tawfīq*).
   - This instills supreme humility in the believer: never look down upon others with spiritual pride; recognize that your faith is an unearned gift of divine mercy.
2. **The Condemnation of Abandoning Reason (*Al-Rijs ʿalā alladhīna Lā Yaʿqilūn*):**
   - *Al-Rijs*: In classical Arabic, *rijs* denotes filthy, foul, repulsive matter; spiritually, it signifies confusion, doubt, hypocrisy, moral perversion, and divine wrath.
   - Notice the extraordinary Qur'anic pairing: God places spiritual defilement upon those who **refuse to use their God-given intellect (*lā yaʿqilūn*)**!

**Classical Exegesis & The Harmony of Faith and Reason in Islam**

Classical scholars and theologians, including Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, al-Ṭabarī, and Ibn Kathīr, highlight that this verse establishes the foundational epistemology of Islam:
- Islam never demands blind, irrational faith; it demands deep intellectual engagement, rational contemplation of cosmic signs, and the rigorous examination of moral truth.
- Disbelief, polytheism, and moral decay are not the result of "too much reasoning"; they are the direct consequence of **abandoning sound reason** in favor of blind cultural conformity, superstition, and unchecked emotional passions.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 100 provides an essential compass for modern intellectual life:
1. **The Islamic Integration of Intellect and Faith:** In Western post-Enlightenment philosophy, faith and reason are often depicted as hostile enemies. In the Qur'anic paradigm, faith and reason are intimate allies: reason is the vehicle that leads human consciousness to the threshold of divine revelation, and divine grace illuminates the intellect.
2. **The Cure for Ideological Blindness:** Spiritual defilement (*Rijs*) falls upon those who turn off their critical thinking and blindly follow political parties, social media trends, or cultural mobs. Islam calls humanity to intellectual liberation through disciplined, God-conscious reasoning (*ʿAql*).

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:101

> **Say, “Behold that which is in the heavens and the earth!” But neither signs nor warners avail a people who believe not.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 101 issues a sweeping, universal divine mandate commanding humanity to engage in rigorous, empirical, and contemplative observation of the cosmos: *"Say, 'Behold that which is in the heavens and the earth!' But neither signs nor warners avail a people who believe not"* (*Quli unẓurū mādhā fī al-samāwāti wa'l-arḍi wa-mā tughnī al-āyātu wa'l-nudhuru ʿan qawmin lā yuʾminūn*).

As Sūrah Yūnus approaches its grand, triumphant conclusion, the divine discourse returns to its central cosmological and epistemological theme: the universe is not an accidental, silent, meaningless machine; it is an open, illuminated theater of divine signs (*Āyāt*) testifying to the supreme intelligence, boundless power, and infinite artistry of the Creator.

However, the verse couples this scientific and spiritual mandate with a sobering psychological verdict: for those who have made an ideological decision to shut their hearts, no amount of cosmic evidence will ever suffice.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Mandate for Cosmic Observation (Al-Naẓar)**

The linguistic architecture of the verse provides profound epistemological depth:
- **The Imperative of Disciplined Observation (*Quli Unẓurū*):**
  - The imperative verb *unẓurū* is not a casual, superficial glance. In Qur'anic epistemology, *al-Naẓar* denotes purposeful, disciplined observation combined with rational deduction and spiritual contemplation (*al-Taʾammul wa'l-Istidlāl*).
  - **In the Heavens (*fī al-samāwāt*):** The vast architectural precision of galaxies, the gravitational calibration of planetary orbits, stellar fusion, the illuminating phases of the moon, and atmospheric physics.
  - **In the Earth (*wa'l-arḍ*):** The geological stability of mountain roots, the biodiversity of ecosystems, the microscopic complexity of cellular biology, the hydrological cycle of water, and the chemical properties supporting conscious life.
- **The Impotence of Evidence Against Obstinate Disbelief (*Wa-mā Tughnī al-Āyātu wa'l-Nudhur...*):**
  - *Al-Āyāt*: The cosmic and scriptural signs.
  - *Al-Nudhur*: The prophetic warners and messengers.
  - *Lā tughnī*: Does not avail, benefit, or convince in the slightest.
  - If an individual has locked their heart in arrogance, cynicism, or fear of moral accountability, the greatest telescopes, the finest logical arguments, and the most eloquent prophets cannot force belief into an unwilling soul.

**Classical Exegesis & The Scientific Methodology of the Qur'an**

Classical scholars and scientists in the Islamic Golden Age, such as Ibn al-Haytham, al-Bīrūnī, and Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, drew foundational inspiration from this verse:
- Verse 101 establishes that the study of the physical universe (natural sciences, astronomy, geology, biology) is a sacred religious act of worship and contemplation (*Tafakkur*).
- The natural world is God's **Created Book (*al-Kitāb al-Manẓūr*)**, while the Qur'an is God's **Revealed Book (*al-Kitāb al-Masṭūr*)**. True science and authentic revelation can never contradict one another, because both originate from the same Divine Author.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 101 provides a vital critique of modern secular scientism:
1. **The Separation of Science from Awe:** Modern secular education teaches science purely as mechanical manipulation for industrial profit, stripping the cosmos of transcendent meaning. Verse 101 invites humanity to re-enchant science: look upon the double helix of DNA or the pillars of a nebula with deep spiritual awe, recognizing the signatures of the Creator.
2. **The Disease of Willful Blindness:** The failure of atheism is not a lack of evidence; the cosmos is overflowing with signs (*Āyāt*). The failure lies in the internal spiritual orientation of the observer. Sincere intellectual inquiry requires an open, humble heart.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:102

> **Do they then await aught save the like of the days of those who passed away before them? Say, “Wait, then; I am with you among those who wait.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 102 poses a piercing rhetorical question to obstinate deniers, confronting them with the historical reality of civilizational collapse: *"Do they then await aught save the like of the days of those who passed away before them? Say, 'Wait, then; I am with you among those who wait'"* (*Fa-hal yantaẓirūna illā mithla ayyāmi alladhīna khalaw min qablihim qul fa'ntaẓirū innī maʿakum mina al-muntaẓirīn*).

Having established the clarity of cosmic signs in verse 101, the Qur'an confronts the Makkan aristocracy and every arrogant civilization with their historical delusion: *By persisting in the exact same arrogance, cruelty, exploitation, and mockery that destroyed ancient empires, are you simply waiting for the exact same catastrophic retribution to strike your society?*

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Days of God (Ayyām Allāh)**

The linguistic phrasing carries profound historical and theological weight:
- **The Rhetorical Interrogative (*Fa-hal Yantaẓirūna Illā...*):** The construction *hal... illā* forms an emphatic rhetorical negation: "They are awaiting nothing whatsoever except the exact fate of fallen empires!"
- **The Concept of *Ayyām* (The Days of Historical Reckoning):**
  - In classical Arabic and Qur'anic usage, *Ayyām Allāh* ("the Days of God") refers to the decisive historical turning points and moments of civilizational reckoning when divine justice obliterated ancient tyrannical powers:
    - The Great Deluge of Noah,
    - The screaming hurricane that shattered ʿĀd,
    - The seismic blast that leveled Thamūd,
    - The crashing sea that swallowed Pharaoh.
- **The Prophetic Ultimatum of Mutual Waiting (*Fa'ntaẓirū Innī Maʿakum...*):**
  - The imperative *intaẓirū* ("Wait, then!") is a calm, serene prophetic ultimatum.
  - The disbelievers wait in cynical anticipation, hoping the Prophet's mission will fail and their monopolies will endure.
  - The Prophet ﷺ waits with absolute certitude (*al-Yaqīn*) in God's unshakeable promise: the inevitable triumph of monotheism, the liberation of the oppressed, and the historical vindication of truth.

**Classical Exegesis & The Immutability of Historical Laws (Sunan al-Taʾrīkh)**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and Ibn Khaldūn in his *Muqaddimah*, emphasize that history operates according to immutable divine laws:
- Societies that build their empires upon injustice, moral decay, and defiance of God contain the seeds of their own inevitable destruction.
- The Quraysh were not exempt from the cosmic laws that destroyed the titans of ʿĀd and Pharaoh.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 102 provides an essential warning for modern civilizations:
1. **The Hubris of Modern Empires:** Modern superpowers often believe that their technological dominance, nuclear arsenals, and economic wealth make them immune to historical collapse. Verse 102 shatters this illusion: no empire is exempt from the moral laws of the cosmos. When a society institutionalizes oppression and moral perversion, it is merely awaiting its *Ayyām*—its day of civilizational reckoning.
2. **The Serenity of Prophetic Confidence:** True believers do not despair when facing powerful opposition. The believer stands with calm, dignified patience, knowing that the arc of history bends inevitably toward divine justice.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:103

> **Then We save Our messengers and those who believe. Thus is it a duty incumbent upon Us: We save the believers.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 103 reveals the eternal, immutable divine pattern (*Sunnat Allāh*) that governs the climax of every prophetic confrontation across human history: *"Then We save Our messengers and those who believe. Thus is it a duty incumbent upon Us: We save the believers"* (*Thumma nunajjī rusulanā wa'lladhīna āmanū kadhālika ḥaqqan ʿalaynā nunji al-muʾminīn*).

Following the warning of civilizational destruction in verse 102, verse 103 unveils the glorious law of divine deliverance: whenever divine retribution descended upon a corrupt society, God miraculously extracted, protected, and elevated His messengers and the righteous believers. No divine catastrophe has ever wiped out the righteous alongside the wicked.

The verse concludes with one of the most comforting, majestic, and sacred declarations in the entire Holy Qur'an: God voluntarily binds Himself by an eternal covenant to save the believers.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Sacred Covenant (Ḥaqqan ʿAlaynā)**

The linguistic architecture of the verse carries supreme theological grandeur:
- **The Deliverance of the Prophetic Chain (*Nunajjī Rusulanā*):** The present-tense imperfect verb *nunajjī* expresses continuous, ongoing divine protection across all generations of history:
  - Noah was delivered aboard the Ark while the earth drowned;
  - Hūd was shielded while the hurricane destroyed ʿĀd;
  - Ṣāliḥ was preserved while the blast shattered Thamūd;
  - Lot was evacuated before Sodom was overturned;
  - Moses and the Israelites crossed the parted sea while Pharaoh drowned;
  - The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ and his companions were granted total victory over Makkah.
- **The Self-Imposed Divine Covenant (*Kadhālika Ḥaqqan ʿAlaynā*):**
  - In Arabic theology and grammar, the phrase *ḥaqqan ʿalaynā* ("a binding duty incumbent upon Us") is extraordinary:
    - Nothing in creation can impose an obligation upon God Almighty; He is the absolute Sovereign Lord.
    - Yet, out of His boundless grace, mercy, and love for the righteous, God **voluntarily obligated upon Himself** an eternal covenant: He will never abandon, forsake, or allow the ultimate destruction of the sincere believers (*Nunji al-Muʾminīn*).

**Classical Exegesis & The Multi-Tiered Dimensions of Salvation (Najāt)**

Classical scholars and theologians, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, unpack the profound dimensions of *Najāt* (salvation):
1. **Salvation in This World (*fī al-Dunyā*):** Protection from civilizational annihilation, spiritual fortitude during persecution, inner peace (*Sakīnah*) amidst worldly chaos, and ultimate historical vindication.
2. **Salvation at the Moment of Death:** Luminous angels descending with the glad tidings of Paradise, shielding the soul from terror.
3. **Salvation in the Hereafter (*fī al-Ākhirah*):** Safety on the Day of Judgment, shelter under the Throne of God, acquittal at the Scale (*al-Mīzān*), safe crossing over the *Ṣirāṭ*, and eternal entry into the Abode of Peace.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 103 provides the ultimate anchor of hope for every believer:
1. **Unshakeable Morale in Times of Crisis:** When believers endure personal hardship, illness, social persecution, or political oppression, this verse is an unbreakable shield of comfort: *Ḥaqqan ʿalaynā nunji al-muʾminīn!* God has bound Himself to save the believers. Worldly trials are temporary tests of refinement; eternal salvation is guaranteed.
2. **Cultivating True Faith (Īmān):** The condition for this divine protection is *Īmān*—authentic, sincere faith that translates into moral integrity, prayer, and trust in God. When you fulfill your covenant of faith with God, God fulfills His covenant of salvation with you.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:104

> **Say, “O mankind! If you are in doubt concerning my religion, then I worship not those whom you worship apart from God; but I worship God, Who causes you to die. And I have been commanded to be of the believers,”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 104 initiates the grand, uncompromising concluding manifesto of Sūrah Yūnus: *"Say, 'O mankind! If you are in doubt concerning my religion, then I worship not those whom you worship apart from God; but I worship God, Who causes you to die. And I have been commanded to be of the believers'"* (*Qul yā ayyuhā al-nāsu in kuntum fī shakkin min dīnī fa-lā aʿbudu alladhīna taʿbudūna min dūni Allāhi wa-lākin aʿbudu Allāha alladhī yatawaffākum wa-umirtu an akūna mina al-muʾminīn*).

God commands the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ to address the entire human race (*Yā ayyuhā al-nās*) with absolute theological clarity, emotional independence, and moral courage. If humanity harbors doubts about the truth of Islam, or imagines that through persecution, economic boycotts, or political bribery they can persuade the Prophet to compromise his creed, he delivers a definitive declaration: I will never bow before your false gods; I worship exclusively the Sovereign Master of human mortality.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Master of Mortality**

The linguistic architecture delivers a devastating theological argument:
- **The Universal Address (*Qul Yā Ayyuhā al-Nās*):** The message of Islam is not a narrow tribal or national movement; it is a universal call to all humanity.
- **The Absolute Rejection of Shirk (*Fa-lā Aʿbudu alladhīna Taʿbudūna...*):** The Prophet ﷺ completely disassociates himself from all false deities, idols, celestial bodies, and mortal tyrants.
- **The Definitive Attribute of God (*Alladhī Yatawaffākum* - Who Causes You to Die):**
  - Notice the singular, chilling attribute chosen to define God here: *The One Who takes your souls in death!*
  - The polytheists worshipped stone statues, stars, and mortal kings that could not grant a single second of life nor take a single soul away.
  - The Prophet ﷺ reminds them: the One I worship is the Omnipotent Sovereign Who holds the timer of your biological existence. At any second, He can terminate your heartbeat, extract your soul, and summon you to His courtroom!
  - How can a mortal human being worship helpless, created objects while ignoring the Sovereign Master Who holds their very life and breath?
- **The Divine Mandate (*Wa-Umirtu an Akūna mina al-Muʾminīn*):**
  - The Prophet's submission is not a personal opinion; it is an absolute divine command from the Lord of the heavens and the earth.

**Classical Exegesis & The Psychology of Confrontation**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, unpack the profound psychological power of this verse:
- Reminding arrogant opponents of their mortality (*yatawaffākum*) instantly disarms their pride.
- No matter how powerful a tyrant or billionaire may be, they cannot escape the Angel of Death. Worship belongs exclusively to the One Who controls mortality.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 104 provides a masterclass in Muslim identity and confidence:
1. **Unapologetic Proclamation of Faith:** In modern pluralistic societies, Muslims are often pressured to water down their creed, apologize for traditional values, or blend into secular consumerism. Verse 104 teaches unyielding dignity: proclaim your monotheism with clarity and kindness, without compromising divine truth.
2. **Remembering the Master of Mortality:** When confronting corrupt systems or intimidating opponents, remember that God holds their souls in His hand (*Alladhī yatawaffākum*). Fearing mortals is irrational when the Creator controls their every breath.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:105

> **and: “Set thy face toward religion as a Ḥanīf, and be not of the polytheists;**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 105 articulates the essential spiritual orientation, psychological focus, and pure monotheistic posture required of the human soul: *"and: 'Set thy face toward religion as a Ḥanīf, and be not of the polytheists'"* (*Wa-an aqim wajhaka li'l-dīni ḥanīfan wa-lā takūnanna mina al-mushrikīn*).

Building directly upon the public manifesto of verse 104, this verse describes the internal and external alignment of the believer: establishing the entire self—intellect, heart, desires, and physical limbs—exclusively toward the straight religion (*al-Dīn al-Qayyim*) in the primordial tradition of Prophet Abraham (*al-Ḥanīfiyyah*).

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Posture of the Soul**

The linguistic phrasing contains profound spiritual and rhetorical precision:
- **Setting the Face (*Aqim Wajhaka li'l-Dīn*):**
  - In Arabic rhetoric, "setting the face" (*Iqāmat al-Wajh*) signifies directing one's entire attention, intention, and life purpose toward a single goal without wavering, distraction, or compromise.
  - Just as a dedicated traveler fixes their gaze straight ahead upon their distant destination without being distracted by roadside illusions, the believer aligns their entire existence exclusively toward the pleasure of God.
- **The Primordial Essence of the *Ḥanīf*:**
  - The root *ḥ-n-f* in Arabic literally denotes turning away from crookedness, deviation, and falsehood, and leaning firmly, permanently toward the straight path of pure monotheism.
  - A *Ḥanīf* is one whose soul is permanently magnetized to the One True God, naturally repelling all forms of idolatry, superstition, and spiritual corruption—embodying the pure spiritual legacy of Prophet Abraham (peace be upon him).
- **The Emphatic Prohibition Against Shirk (*Wa-lā Takūnanna mina al-Mushrikīn*):**
  - The heavy emphatic *nūn* (*lā takūnanna*) delivers an absolute, uncompromising warning against all forms of *Shirk*:
    - Major polytheism (*al-Shirk al-Akbar*): Directing worship, prayers, or sacrifices to created entities.
    - Minor polytheism (*al-Shirk al-Aṣghar* / *al-Riyāʾ*): Showing off in worship, seeking human approval, or loving worldly wealth more than God.

**Classical Exegesis & The Spiritual Legacy of Abraham**

Classical scholars, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and al-Qurṭubī, highlight that *al-Ḥanīfiyyah* is the timeless spiritual core of all prophetic messages:
- Prophet Abraham was the archetypal *Ḥanīf*: he stood alone against his father's idol-carving workshop, the tyrannical empire of Nimrod, and the celestial cults of his people, setting his face exclusively to the Creator: *"Indeed, I have turned my face toward Him Who created the heavens and the earth, as a Ḥanīf, and I am not of the polytheists"* (6:79).

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 105 provides an essential guide for navigating modern spiritual distractions:
1. **Singleness of Purpose in a Distracted World:** Modern human beings suffer from chronic mental fragmentation—pulled in thousands of directions by consumer desires, social media notifications, and career anxieties. Verse 105 calls for spiritual focus: *Aqim wajhaka!* Unify your life purpose around the worship of your Creator.
2. **Purifying the Heart from Hidden Idolatry:** Modern shirk often takes subtle psychological forms: worshipping one's own ego (*Hawā*), obsessing over social validation, or placing ultimate security in bank accounts. The *Ḥanīf* continuously purges these hidden idols, keeping the heart pure for God alone.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:106

> **and call not, apart from God, upon that which neither benefits thee nor harms thee. For if thou dost so, thou wilt then surely be among the wrongdoers.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 106 delivers a definitive, universal theological prohibition against invoking or worshipping any created entity: *"and call not, apart from God, upon that which neither benefits thee nor harms thee. For if thou dost so, thou wilt then surely be among the wrongdoers"* (*Wa-lā tadʿu min dūni Allāhi mā lā yanfaʿuka wa-lā yaḍurruka fa-in faʿalta fa-innaka idhan mina al-ẓālimīn*).

The verse establishes the fundamental Qur'anic criterion of divinity: **effective, independent agency**. Any entity that lacks the sovereign power to independently bestow benefit (*Nafʿ*) or avert harm (*Ḍarr*) is ontologically disqualified from being an object of prayer (*Duʿāʾ*), worship, or ultimate reliance.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Criterion of Effective Agency**

The linguistic architecture delivers an unyielding logical and spiritual boundary:
- **The Prohibition of Supplication (*Wa-lā Tadʿu min Dūni Allāh*):**
  - The verb *daʿā* in Qur'anic terminology encompasses supplication, prayer, seeking supernatural aid (*al-Istighāthah*), and worship (*al-ʿIbādah*).
  - The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ declared:
    > *"Supplication (al-Duʿāʾ) is the very essence of worship."* (Jāmiʿ al-Tirmidhī 3247; Sunan Abī Dāwūd 1479, graded ṣaḥīḥ)
  - Directing supplication to anything other than God is a direct act of major polytheism (*Shirk*).
- **The Absolute Impotence of Creation (*Mā Lā Yanfaʿuka wa-Lā Yaḍurruk*):**
  - Every single created entity—whether an ancient stone statue, a deceased human being in a grave, an angel, a celestial planet, or a modern political superpower—possesses zero autonomous power to bring a single drop of benefit or inflict a speck of harm without God's permission.
- **The Severe Verdict: Categorization as Ẓālimīn (*Fa-innaka Idhan mina al-Ẓālimīn*):**
  - If a person invokes created entities, they are stamped as being among the *Ẓālimūn* (the wrongdoers/unjust).
  - In Qur'anic theology, *al-Ẓulm* at its highest level is *al-Shirk*, as Luqmān advised his son: *"Verily, associating partners with God is indeed a tremendous injustice"* (31:13). *Ẓulm* literally means placing something in other than its rightful place: taking the sacred devotion that belongs exclusively to the Creator and directing it to helpless dust.

**Classical Exegesis & Pedagogical Address to the Prophet ﷺ**

Classical commentators, including al-Ṭabarī, Ibn Kathīr, and Fakhr al-Dīn al-Rāzī, explain that although this warning is addressed grammatically to the Prophet ﷺ, its purpose is pedagogical for all humanity:
- The Prophet ﷺ is absolutely protected from *Shirk* (*Maʿṣūm*).
- If the greatest prophet is warned that invoking creation would classify him as a wrongdoer, how much more severe is the warning for ordinary human beings!

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 106 provides the ultimate liberation of human consciousness:
1. **Total Freedom from Superstition:** Islam frees humanity from the fear of curses, evil eyes, horoscopes, lucky charms, and superstitious shrines. No created entity can harm you or benefit you without the decree of the Lord of the worlds.
2. **Direct, Unmediated Access to the Divine:** You do not need gatekeepers, deceased intermediaries, or priests to reach God. Raise your hands anywhere on earth, and the King of the universe hears your prayer directly.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:107

> **And if God touches thee with an affliction, there is none to remove it save He; and if He desires any good for thee, there is none to repel His Bounty. He causes it to reach whomsoever He will of His servants. And He is the Forgiving, the Merciful.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 107 is one of the most liberating, comforting, and transformative spiritual declarations in the entire Holy Qur'an: *"And if God touches thee with an affliction, there is none to remove it save He; and if He desires any good for thee, there is none to repel His Bounty. He causes it to reach whomsoever He will of His servants. And He is the Forgiving, the Merciful"* (*Wa-in yamsaska Allāhu bi-ḍurrin fa-lā kāshifa lahu illā huwa wa-in yuridka bi-khayrin fa-lā rādda li-faḍlihi yuṣību bihi man yashāʾu min ʿibādihi wa-huwa al-ghafūru al-raḥīm*).

This verse articulates the absolute divine monopoly over harm, tribulation, bounty, and deliverance. It completely liberates the human heart from paralyzing anxiety, superstitious fears, and subservience to mortal creatures, anchoring the soul in the unshakeable certainty of divine sovereignty and boundless mercy.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Asymmetry of Affliction and Bounty**

The linguistic architecture of the verse contains exquisite theological precision:

1. **The Light Touch of Affliction (*Wa-in Yamsaska Allāhu bi-Ḍurr...*):**
   - The verb *massa* denotes touching lightly. Even the greatest worldly catastrophe—a terminal illness, financial collapse, bereavement, or societal crisis—is merely a light touch (*ḍurr*) in the cosmic scale.
   - *Fa-lā kāshifa lahu illā Huw* ("there is none to remove it save He"): The verb *kashafa* denotes lifting, peeling away, or curing. When an affliction touches a human by divine decree, all the physicians, governments, scientists, and wealth in the universe cannot lift that trial by a single millimeter until God decrees it.
2. **The Irresistible Downpour of Divine Bounty (*Fa-lā Rādda li-Faḍlih*):**
   - Notice the exquisite linguistic difference:
     - Affliction is described as a touch (*yamsaska*).
     - Good is described as an active divine will (*yuridka bi-khayr*) and boundless bounty (*Faḍl*).
   - *Fa-lā rādda li-faḍlih* ("there is none to repel His Bounty"): The noun *rādd* preceded by the categorical negative *lā* (*lā al-nāfiyah li'l-jins*) means that when God decrees guidance, provision, honor, health, or salvation for a servant, all the jealous critics, corrupt enemies, and obstacles in the universe combined cannot block a single droplet of that divine favor from reaching its intended recipient!
3. **The Divine Attributes of Solace (*Huwa al-Ghafūru al-Raḥīm*):**
   - The verse seals its message with *al-Ghafūr* (The All-Forgiving) and *al-Raḥīm* (The Boundlessly Merciful), reminding the believer that even trials and afflictions are forms of concealed mercy designed to purify sins, elevate spiritual ranks, and draw the soul closer to its Lord.

**Authentic Prophetic Traditions on Divine Sovereignty**

The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ immortalized the essence of this verse in his famous, foundational advice to ʿAbdullāh ibn ʿAbbās:

> *"Know that if the entire nation were to gather together to benefit you with something, they could not benefit you except with something that God had already decreed for you; and if they were to gather together to harm you with something, they could not harm you except with something that God had already decreed against you. The pens have been lifted and the pages have dried!"* (Jāmiʿ al-Tirmidhī 2516, graded ṣaḥīḥ)

The Prophet ﷺ also recited after every obligatory prayer:

> *"O God! None can withhold what You have given, and none can give what You have withheld, and the wealth of the wealthy person cannot benefit him against You!"* (Ṣaḥīḥ al-Bukhārī 844; Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim 593)

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 107 provides the ultimate psychological therapy for the modern soul:
1. **Total Liberation from Human Fear and Jealousy:** When you internalize that no human being can block the bounty (*Faḍl*) that God has written for you, envy (*ḥasad*) and anxiety evaporate from your heart. You stop fearing corporate rivals, corrupt bosses, or critics, knowing that your destiny is held exclusively by the Loving Creator.
2. **Patience in Tribulation:** When hardship strikes, the believer does not despair or panic. They turn directly to the Only One Who possesses the power to lift it (*fa-lā kāshifa lahu illā Huw*), finding peace in prayer, repentance, and patience.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:108

> **Say, “O mankind! The truth has come unto you from your Lord. So whosoever is guided is guided only for [the good of] his own soul, and whosoever goes astray goes astray only against it. And I am not a keeper over you.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 108 delivers the penultimate universal proclamation of Sūrah Yūnus, summarizing the entire prophetic mission, the reality of moral autonomy, and the absolute independence of divine majesty: *"Say, 'O mankind! The truth has come unto you from your Lord. So whosoever is guided is guided only for [the good of] his own soul, and whosoever goes astray goes astray only against it. And I am not a keeper over you'"* (*Qul yā ayyuhā al-nāsu qad jāʾakumu al-ḥaqqu min Rabbikum fa-mani ihtadā fa-innamā yahtadī li-nafsihi wa-man ḍalla fa-innamā yaḍillu ʿalayhā wa-mā anā ʿalaykum bi-wakīl*).

The Qur'an, the prophetic biography, and the cosmic signs have been unveiled with absolute clarity. Humanity can no longer plead ignorance. The verse establishes the foundational Qur'anic law of spiritual cause and effect: human choices affect only the human soul; God is completely Self-Sufficient (*al-Ghanī*).

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Autonomy of Moral Consequence**

The linguistic architecture delivers two symmetrical, unshakeable equations:
- **The Arrival of Objective Truth (*Qad Jāʾakumu al-Ḥaqqu min Rabbikum*):** The confirmatory *qad* combined with *al-Ḥaqq* affirms that truth is not an abstract theory; it has arrived as a concrete, historical reality in the Qur'an and the Prophet ﷺ.
- **Equation 1: The Fruit of Guidance (*Fa-mani Ihtadā fa-Innamā Yahtadī li-Nafsih*):**
  - Whosoever accepts the guidance, cleanses their heart, prays, and lives righteously benefits only their own eternal soul.
  - God does not gain anything from our obedience; our prostrations do not expand His kingdom by an atom.
- **Equation 2: The Self-Destruction of Error (*Wa-man Ḍalla fa-Innamā Yaḍillu ʿAlayhā*):**
  - Whosoever chooses cynicism, arrogance, and moral corruption harms only their own soul (*ʿalayhā*).
  - Human disbelief does not diminish God's sovereignty; the denier simply sets fire to their own eternal future.
- **The Messenger's Role: Conveyor, Not Compelling Warden (*Wa-mā Anā ʿAlaykum bi-Wakīl*):**
  - The term *wakīl* denotes a guardian, custodian, or trustee who is legally and physically responsible for compelling someone's behavior.
  - The Prophet Muhammad ﷺ is commissioned to deliver the message with love and clarity; he is not an authoritarian warden sent to force compliance upon human hearts.

**Classical Exegesis & The Ḥadīth Qudsī on Divine Self-Sufficiency**

Imam Muslim records in his *Ṣaḥīḥ* the monumental *Ḥadīth Qudsī* that perfectly illuminates this verse:

> *"O My servants! If the first of you and the last of you, the humans among you and the jinn among you, were all as pious as the most pious heart of any single man among you, that would not increase My dominion in the slightest. O My servants! If the first of you and the last of you, the humans among you and the jinn among you, were all as wicked as the most wicked heart of any single man among you, that would not decrease My dominion in the slightest!"* (Ṣaḥīḥ Muslim 2577, from Abū Dharr al-Ghifārī)

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 108 provides an essential framework for personal responsibility and daʿwah:
1. **Radical Individual Moral Responsibility:** You are the ultimate author of your eternal destiny. You cannot blame your parents, society, or culture for your spiritual choices. If you choose guidance, you save yourself; if you choose misguidance, you destroy yourself.
2. **The Freedom of the Caller to Truth:** The advocate of Islam is liberated from emotional burnout: share the message with beauty and excellence, knowing that *wa-mā anā ʿalaykum bi-wakīl*—you are a conveyor of light, not a warden of souls.

---
## Sūrah Yūnus 10:109

> **And follow that which is revealed unto thee, and be patient till God shall judge; and He is the Best of judges.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Context, Asbāb al-Nuzūl & Thematic Architecture**

Verse 109 brings Sūrah Yūnus to its majestic, awe-inspiring conclusion, encapsulating the entire prophetic methodology, spiritual pathway, and cosmic outlook of Islam in two foundational divine commands and a supreme affirmation of divine justice: *"And follow that which is revealed unto thee, and be patient till God shall judge; and He is the Best of judges"* (*Wa'ttabiʿ mā yūḥā ilayka wa'ṣbir ḥattā yaḥkuma Allāhu wa-huwa khayru al-ḥākimīn*).

Having traversed the entirety of cosmic creation, the psychology of human ingratitude, the legal realities of revelation, the grand narratives of Noah, Moses, and Jonah, and the immutable laws of civilizational rise and fall, the divine discourse seals the Sūrah by providing the ultimate marching orders for the Prophet Muhammad ﷺ and every believer until the end of time.

**Linguistic, Lexical & Syntactic Analysis: The Two Pillars of Prophetic Methodology**

The linguistic architecture delivers two foundational imperatives:

1. **Adherence to Divine Revelation (*Wa'ttabiʿ Mā Yūḥā Ilayk*):**
   - The Form VIII imperative *ittabiʿ* denotes actively, closely, and consistently following in the footsteps of revelation (*al-Waḥy*).
   - The Prophet ﷺ and every believer are commanded to make the revealed Word of God the absolute compass and governing criterion of their lives—in creed, morality, family relations, economic justice, and political governance—unswayed by worldly fashions, public opinion polls, or the hostility of critics.
2. **Embodying Steadfast Patience (*Wa'ṣbir*):**
   - Patience (*al-Ṣabr*) is the indispensable companion of faith. The believer must exercise *Ṣabr* across three comprehensive dimensions:
     - **Patience in Obedience (*al-Ṣabr ʿalā al-Ṭāʿah*):** Steadfastness in daily prayers, charity, and righteous deeds;
     - **Patience in Avoiding Sin (*al-Ṣabr ʿan al-Maʿṣiyah*):** Resisting corrupt temptations and worldly lusts;
     - **Patience in Tribulation (*al-Ṣabr ʿalā Aqdār Allāh*):** Enduring persecutions, slanders, and worldly hardships with dignity, hope, and emotional composure.

**The Eschatological Climax: Waiting Upon the Infallible Judge (Ḥattā Yaḥkuma Allāh)**

The verse instructs the Prophet ﷺ to persevere with serene confidence: *"till God shall judge"* (*ḥattā yaḥkuma Allāh*).
- The struggles between truth and falsehood, justice and oppression, are not decided by human arrogance, media propaganda, or military weapons; they are resolved by the sovereign decree of the Almighty.
- The believer works tirelessly for truth, leaving the final historical outcome in the hands of the Lord of the worlds.

**The Ultimate Divine Title: He is the Best of Judges (Khayru al-Ḥākimīn)**

The Sūrah reaches its triumphant conclusion with one of the most magnificent divine names: *"and He is the Best of judges"* (*Wa-Huwa Khayru al-Ḥākimīn*).
- God is *Khayr al-Ḥākimīn* because:
  1. **His Knowledge is Absolute:** He knows every hidden motive, secret conspiracy, and whispered word;
  2. **His Justice is Flawless:** He never commits an atom's weight of injustice (*Ẓulm*);
  3. **His Verdicts are Irresistible:** When He passes judgment, no corrupt court, military power, or tyrant can appeal, delay, or overturn His sovereign decree.

**Spiritual Psychology, Philosophical Depth & Contemporary Application**

Verse 109 provides the ultimate conclusion and daily spiritual compass for the modern believer:
1. **The Formula for Victorious Living:** Follow Revelation (*Ittibāʿ al-Waḥy*) and Practice Patience (*al-Ṣabr*). When a human being unites divine guidance with steadfast patience, they become spiritually invincible amidst worldly storms.
2. **Serene Trust in Ultimate Justice:** Modern human beings often feel overwhelmed by the apparent triumph of corruption and cruelty in global affairs. Sūrah Yūnus closes with unshakeable serenity: *Wa-Huwa Khayru al-Ḥākimīn!* The Supreme Judge is on the Throne, absolute justice will be rendered, and the final victory belongs to the righteous.
